327 — AI Agent Memory Architecture

Project Number: 327

Project Name: AI Agent Memory Architecture

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

Goal: Implement, from scratch in Rust, an AI agent memory architecture: a durable store (append-only WAL + record store, mmap, crash recovery, compaction), a multi-index retrieval layer (HNSW vector ANN + BM25 inverted index + recency + importance) with hybrid RRF fusion, MMR diversity and a rerank hook, a write/ingest pipeline (LLM-iface fact/entity extraction, dedup, embed), background consolidation/reflection, decay/forgetting with hot/warm/cold tiering, a context-window assembler, and the agent-facing remember()/recall()/forget() API across working/episodic/semantic/procedural memory kinds (crates mem-core/store/index/embed/ ingest/retrieve/consolidate/forget/context/api). The LLM is abstracted behind LlmIface; it is not an agent framework or a general-purpose vector DB. Validated by retrieval-quality fixtures and differential tests against brute-force kNN.

1. Goal

1.1 Engineering Definition

Build a production-grade, durable memory subsystem for LLM agents, implemented in Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78). Delivers: crash-safe record store (WAL + mmap), multi-index hybrid retrieval (vector ANN + BM25 + recency + importance), write/consolidation pipeline (extract → embed → dedup → store → consolidate), forgetting/tiering, and the agent-facing remember() / recall() API. The subsystem calls out to an abstract LlmIface for summarization, entity extraction, embedding, and reranking — it is not, and does not contain, an LLM.

1.2 System Boundaries

Inside scope Outside scope
Durable record store (WAL, mmap, compaction, crash recovery) LLM inference engine or model weights
Multi-index retrieval (HNSW, BM25, recency, importance) Agent planner / tool-use / orchestration framework
Write pipeline: observation → extract → chunk → embed → dedup → store General-purpose vector database (HNSW is internal; not exposed as standalone service)
Consolidation pipeline: summarize / reflect / merge / link Embedding model training or fine-tuning
Forgetting: decay, TTL, eviction, hot/warm/cold tiering, archival External HTTP / gRPC API server (mem-api exposes Rust public surface only)
Context-window assembler: token budgeting, packing, prompt assembly Multi-node distributed consensus or replication
Session and namespace management Cross-agent memory sharing (namespaces isolate; federation is PhaseDeferred("15.x"))
Telemetry (metrics + tracing) Real-time streaming / streaming vector updates beyond buffer flush

1.3 Explicit Non-Goals

  • No embedded LLM: summarize/extract/embed/rerank delegated to LlmIface trait.
  • No general vector DB: HNSW is internal to mem-index; no external query protocol.
  • No agent orchestration: memory subsystem provides primitives; orchestration is the caller's concern.
  • No distributed consensus: single-node, single-writer WAL; replication is PhaseDeferred("16.x").
  • No multi-tenant RBAC: namespace isolation scoped per agent session; auth is PhaseDeferred("17.x").
  • No streaming ingest API: observation buffer + async flush; push-based streaming is PhaseDeferred("18.x").
  • No cross-agent memory federation: PhaseDeferred("15.x") (same deferred feature as §1.2's cross-namespace sharing note).

1.4 Intended Deployment Model

  • Embedded library: linked into an agent process; mem-store owns local storage directory.
  • Local-disk persistence: WAL and record store on POSIX filesystem; mmap for read hot-path.
  • Single writer, concurrent readers: write pipeline serialized via channel; retrieval concurrent via shared read lock on index snapshots.
  • Optional remote embedding backend: mem-embed talks to local or remote embedding server via LlmIface; latency budget enforced at ingestion.
  • Horizontal scale (future): read replicas via snapshot export; PhaseDeferred("16.x").

1.5 Target Users / Operators / Developers

Role Primary concern
Agent developer remember() / recall() API correctness, latency SLA, context quality
Platform engineer WAL durability, compaction, tiering, observability, crash recovery
ML engineer Retrieval quality (MRR, NDCG), embedding provider swap, reflection quality
Operator Storage budget, eviction policy tuning, telemetry dashboards
Contributor Crate boundaries, trait seam stability, test harness, bench regression

1.6 Phase-0 Deliverable — Crate Manifest

Ordered by dependency (leaf → root):

  1. mem-core — core types: MemoryId, MemoryRecord, MemoryKind, Salience, Embedding, Provenance, Query, RetrievalResult, ContextBudget, ConsolidationJob, NsId, MemError.
  2. mem-store — durable engine: append-only WAL, record store, mmap read path, crash recovery, compaction, snapshots.
  3. mem-index — indices: vector ANN (HNSW), keyword inverted index (BM25), recency/temporal index, importance index, metadata secondary indices.
  4. mem-embed — embedding interface (provider-abstract via LlmIface), chunker, embedding cache.
  5. mem-ingest — write path: capture → fact/entity extraction (LlmIface) → dedup → embed → store; observation buffer.
  6. mem-retrieve — read path: hybrid retrieval (vector + BM25 + recency + importance), fusion (RRF), MMR diversity, rerank hook.
  7. mem-consolidate — background: summarization, reflection (higher-order memories), merge/dedup, link-graph maintenance.
  8. mem-forget — decay model, eviction policy, TTL, hot/warm/cold tiering, archival.
  9. mem-context — working memory + context-window assembler: token budgeting, packing, eviction working→long-term, prompt assembly.
  10. mem-api — public surface: remember(), recall(), forget(), reflect(); session/namespace management.
  11. mem-telemetry — metrics + tracing: taps all crates via tracing + metrics facades.
  12. mem-cli — driver binary: interactive + batch mode; introspection, replay, migration.
  13. mem-bench — retrieval-quality harness (MRR, NDCG, Recall@K) + soak test (throughput, latency percentiles).

1.7 What Later Phases Unlock

Phase range Capability unlocked
Phase 1–4 Core WAL + record store + basic CRUD + mem-core types
Phase 5–7 HNSW + BM25 + recency/importance indices; hybrid retrieval
Phase 8–9 Write pipeline (ingest + embed + dedup); LlmIface integration
Phase 10–11 Consolidation (summarize, reflect, link-graph); forgetting (decay, TTL, tiering)
Phase 12–13 Context assembler; full remember() / recall() round-trip
Phase 14 Telemetry instrumentation; bench harness; retrieval quality baseline
Phase 15+ (PhaseDeferred) replication, federation, auth, streaming, multi-node

1.8 Deferred Features

  • PhaseDeferred("15.x") — cross-namespace / cross-agent memory federation.
  • PhaseDeferred("16.x") — distributed replication; read-replica snapshot sync.
  • PhaseDeferred("17.x") — multi-tenant RBAC; namespace-level access control.
  • PhaseDeferred("18.x") — push-based streaming ingest; continuous observation stream.
  • PhaseDeferred("19.x") — graph-native retrieval (full knowledge-graph traversal).
  • PhaseDeferred("20.x") — pluggable remote index backend (external ANN service).

1.9 Timeline

  • Phase 0 (crate scaffolding + core types + WAL + basic store): 6–14 weeks, 1–2 engineers.
  • Full implementation (all 13 crates, all phases, bench baseline): 4–9 months, 2–4 engineers.
  • Milestones: WAL durability → index correctness → hybrid retrieval quality → consolidation loop → full API round-trip → operational readiness.

1.10 Recommended Language & Rationale

Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78):

  • WAL + mmap + crash-safety → unsafe mmap with safe wrappers; ownership enforces exclusive write access.
  • High-throughput retrieval + SIMD vector math → std::simd / manual SIMD; zero-copy read path via mmap.
  • Background consolidation + async flush → tokio for async I/O; rayon for parallel vector index construction.
  • Predictable memory → no GC pauses; salience-guided eviction stays deterministic.
  • Safe FFI for embedding/rerank backends → LlmIface trait object; optional C FFI to hnswlib.
  • Rejected Python: storage-engine + concurrency-control concerns favor Rust; ML calls are delegated.

1.11 Required Prerequisites

Domain Required depth
Systems programming Memory layouts, mmap, file I/O, WAL patterns, page cache
Concurrency Rust ownership + Send/Sync; async/await (tokio); reader-writer locks
Storage engines Log-structured storage, compaction, crash recovery, CRC/checksums
Information retrieval Inverted index, BM25, ANN/HNSW, RRF, MMR, recall/precision
ML systems Embedding model APIs, chunking strategies, reranking
Distributed systems Crash-consistency, idempotent replay, snapshot isolation
Rust ecosystem serde, ulid, tracing, metrics, tokio, rayon, memmap2

2. Why Build This

2.1 Systems Knowledge Taught

Skill area Specific technique exercised
Log-structured storage Append-only WAL, segment rotation, CRC integrity, replay-on-boot
mmap + crash safety MAP_SHARED read path, write-through via WAL-before-mmap, msync policy
ANN at practical scale HNSW graph construction (M, ef_construction), search (ef), layer promotion
Information retrieval BM25 term scoring, inverted index with positional gaps, score normalization
Hybrid search fusion RRF score merging across heterogeneous indices; MMR diversity reranking
Consolidation pipelines Async background jobs; summarization-driven knowledge distillation
Decay / forgetting Exponential salience decay; eviction policy (LRU-salience hybrid); tiering
Token budget management Greedy packing, priority-ordered eviction, working-memory flush
Trait-based abstraction LlmIface seam; pluggable embedding provider; pluggable ANN backend
Observability Structured tracing spans, histogram metrics, per-crate instrumentation

2.2 Historical + Architectural Significance

  • Generative Agents (Park 2023): first to combine recency + importance + relevance scores with reflection; this architecture operationalizes that paper's retrieval model.
  • MemGPT / Letta (Packer 2023): tiered working/archival memory; the context-window budgeting in mem-context implements this model durably.
  • HNSW (Malkov & Yashunin 2016): hierarchical navigable small-world graph; standard ANN substrate; building it from-spec gives visceral understanding of graph index tradeoffs.
  • RRF (Cormack 2009): rank-fusion across heterogeneous retrievers; the retrieval layer depends on this directly.
  • HippoRAG 2024: hippocampus-inspired memory association via link-graph; mem-consolidate's link maintenance is motivated here.
  • RAPTOR 2024: hierarchical summarization tree; reflection phases implement a subset of RAPTOR's recursive abstraction.

2.3 Engineering Muscles Developed

  • Write-ahead logging discipline: fsync semantics, durability ack, replay idempotency.
  • Index consistency under concurrent writes: write-lock around HNSW mutation; snapshot-then-search for readers.
  • Embedding pipeline engineering: chunking strategy effects on recall; cache hit rates; provider latency budgets.
  • Retrieval quality measurement: MRR, NDCG@K, Recall@K; building eval harnesses, not just code.
  • Background pipeline correctness: consolidation job replay safety; idempotent summarization; dedup under retries.
  • Memory pressure management: salience-guided eviction; working-memory flush to long-term without data loss.
  • Tokio + Rayon interop: bridging async I/O (ingest/retrieve) with CPU-parallel index construction (rayon).
  • Unsafe Rust discipline: mmap safety invariants documented as # Safety comments; reviewed under Miri.

2.4 Production Engineering Concepts Exercised

  • Durability SLAs: WAL fsync-on-commit vs. group-commit batching; configurable durability policy.
  • Compaction without downtime: generation-based compaction; read path continues during compaction.
  • Graceful degradation: embedding provider timeout → store record without embedding; retrieval falls back to BM25 + recency.
  • Operational introspection: mem-cli WAL replay, index inspection, salience histogram, eviction audit log.
  • Benchmark-driven development: mem-bench regression gates on MRR, p99 latency, throughput.
  • Schema evolution: MemoryRecord versioned via version: u16 field; forward-compatible deserialization.

2.5 Cross-Links to Related Portfolio Projects

Portfolio project Relevant overlap
Storage engine / LSM-tree WAL + compaction patterns directly transferable
Vector database HNSW index construction + ANN search; pluggable backend seam
Compiler / IR Provenance tracking; MemoryId monotonicity analogous to SSA value numbering
Scheduler / task queue ConsolidationJob scheduling; background pipeline dispatch
Consensus / Raft Durability ack patterns; WAL segment sealing mirrors log entry commit
GPU / tensor runtime Embedding computation offload via LlmIface; SIMD dot-product in recall
Networking stack LlmIface HTTP transport; async I/O boundaries; timeout + retry discipline
Full-text search engine BM25 inverted index implementation; tokenization pipeline

2.6 Calibration Value

  • Breadth signal: spans storage, retrieval, ML systems, concurrency, observability — rare in single projects.
  • Depth signal: WAL crash recovery + HNSW correctness + hybrid fusion + consolidation are each independently hard; all in one system is principal-level scope.
  • Differentiation: most portfolios have a vector DB or a storage engine; this is a complete LLM-memory architecture with retrieval quality evaluation.
  • Interview leverage: every subsystem is a natural deep-dive: "walk me through your WAL replay logic" / "how does your RRF fusion work" / "how does salience decay affect eviction".

2.7 What Mastery Looks Like

  • WAL durability discipline (Phase 1 / §13): can design a crash-safe append-only log from scratch — fsync semantics, CRC-per-frame, partial-entry truncation on recovery, group-commit tradeoff; knows when fdatasync suffices vs fsync on parent directory.
  • ANN index fundamentals (Phase 3 / §15): can explain HNSW layer-probability draw, neighbor-selection heuristic, and why tombstone ratio above 15% degrades recall; can derive ef_construction vs recall vs build-time tradeoff quantitatively.
  • Hybrid retrieval engineering (Phase 4 / §16): can implement RRF from Cormack 2009 from scratch, explain why k=60 is robust to score-scale mismatch, and tune recency/importance bias to achieve target MRR on a real corpus.
  • Consolidation pipeline correctness (Phase 8 / §20): understands idempotent consolidation (derived_from dedup), reflection amplification risks, WAL-first ordering for multi-step jobs, and how to replay a partially-committed Summarize job safely.
  • Forgetting and tiering (Phase 9 / §21): can derive the composite decay function from first principles, set λ_r for a target half-life, reason about tiering thresholds given storage costs, and prove no-silent-loss via the eviction audit log.
  • Token-budget assembly (Phase 7 / §19): can implement priority-based greedy packing, detect and handle single-record budget overflow, and design the working→episodic eviction path without deadlock under async constraints.
  • Retrieval quality evaluation (Phase 13 / §25): can set up a differential harness (HNSW vs brute-force oracle), interpret nDCG@K vs MRR vs Recall@K to diagnose index vs fusion vs rerank failures, and design a soak test that catches memory leaks without false positives.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

Show the original text diagram
    agent --remember(obs)--> [mem-ingest] extract facts/entities (LlmIface), dedup, chunk, embed
                                   |  MemoryRecord
                            [mem-store]  WAL + record store (mmap, crash recovery, compaction)
                              |        |          |            |
                       [vec ANN/HNSW][BM25 inverted][recency/temporal][importance]   (mem-index)
                              |________|__________|____________|
                                          |
    agent --recall(query)--> [mem-retrieve] hybrid fusion (RRF) + MMR + rerank hook
                                          |  ranked MemoryRecords
                            [mem-context] token-budget pack -> prompt context --> agent
    background: [mem-consolidate] summarize/reflect/merge/link     [mem-forget] decay/evict/tier/archive
                            [mem-telemetry] taps all paths

3.2 mem-ingest — Write Pipeline Entry Point

  • Responsibilities: accept raw Observation strings; dispatch to LlmIface::extract_facts() and LlmIface::extract_entities(); chunk oversized content; call mem-embed for embedding vector; dedup via content-hash + semantic similarity threshold; write MemoryRecord to mem-store; notify mem-index of new record; push ConsolidationJob to background queue.
  • Concurrency boundary: single-writer channel (tokio::sync::mpsc); multiple callers enqueue Observations; single ingest worker drains the channel sequentially to enforce WAL ordering.
  • Failure modes: LlmIface timeout → store record with embedding_ref: None; dedup collision → return existing MemoryId to caller; store write failure → return MemError::StoreFull / MemError::Io.
  • Invariant: every successfully returned remember() call has a durably fsynced WAL entry before Ok(MemoryId) is returned.

3.3 mem-store — Durable Engine

  • Responsibilities: append-only WAL (segment files, CRC32 per entry, configurable fsync policy); record store (fixed-layout pages, mmap for reads); crash recovery (WAL replay on open, discard trailing partial entries); compaction (background generation sweep, atomic segment swap); snapshots (consistent read view for backup/replica).
  • Persistence boundary: only crate that touches the filesystem (except mem-embed's embedding cache). WAL directory and record-store directory are separate paths; configurable.
  • Concurrency boundary: exclusive write lock via RwLock-protected StoreInner; mmap read path acquires shared lock on active generation; compaction runs on separate thread with generation pointer swap.
  • Invariants: WAL entry order = MemoryId monotonic order; record store is rebuild-from-WAL-capable; compaction never drops a live record; every eviction writes a tombstone to WAL before removal.

3.4 mem-index — Multi-Index Layer

  • Responsibilities: maintain four parallel indices over MemoryRecords: (1) HNSW vector ANN graph (M, ef_construction configurable); (2) BM25 inverted index with per-namespace term statistics; (3) recency/temporal B-tree index keyed by created_at / last_access; (4) importance/salience sorted-set. Plus secondary metadata indices (namespace filter, kind filter, as_of timestamp filter).
  • Protocol boundary: IndexHandle trait exposes insert(), delete(), search(), snapshot(); HNSW backend pluggable (internal pure-Rust impl or FFI to hnswlib).
  • Concurrency boundary: HNSW write serialized; BM25 uses append-only posting-list with background merge; recency and importance indices use BTreeMap behind RwLock.
  • Invariant: index entries and store records never dangle; compaction calls index.delete() atomically with store record deletion; as_of queries filter by created_at ≤ timestamp.

3.5 mem-embed — Embedding Interface + Cache

  • Responsibilities: implement EmbeddingProvider trait backed by LlmIface::embed(); chunk text to model token limit (configurable chunker strategy: sentence / fixed-token / semantic); maintain in-memory LRU embedding cache keyed by content-hash; batch embedding requests for throughput.
  • External interface: LlmIface trait is the only outbound network dependency; implementation can be HTTP REST, gRPC, or local FFI.
  • Failure modes: provider timeout → MemError::EmbedTimeout; dimension mismatch → MemError::DimMismatch; cache miss + provider down → store record without embedding, flag embedding_ref: None, schedule retry via ConsolidationJob.
  • Invariant: embedding dimension D is fixed per namespace at creation; Embedding structs in index are always dimension-D vectors.

3.6 mem-retrieve — Hybrid Retrieval Read Path

  • Responsibilities: accept Query; dispatch in parallel to: HNSW ANN search, BM25 inverted-index search, recency index scan (top-K by last_access + recency_bias), importance index scan (top-K by salience * importance_bias); fuse ranked lists via RRF (k=60 default); apply MMR diversity pass (λ configurable); invoke optional LlmIface::rerank() hook on top-N candidates; return Vec<RetrievalResult>.
  • Concurrency boundary: all four index searches run in parallel (tokio::spawn + join_all); each index provides a snapshot-consistent read handle.
  • Determinism invariant: fixed index state + query → identical ranked set; stable tie-break by score (desc) then MemoryId (asc, ULID order).
  • Failure modes: HNSW search error → fallback to BM25 + recency only; rerank timeout → skip rerank, return RRF-fused result; k > index_size → return all records.

3.7 mem-context — Context-Window Assembler

  • Responsibilities: maintain working-memory ring buffer (MemoryKind::Working, bounded by ContextBudget.max_tokens); accept ranked Vec<RetrievalResult> from mem-retrieve; greedy-pack highest-priority records within token budget (tokenizer via LlmIface::count_tokens()); evict lowest-salience working memories to long-term (mem-ingest write path) when budget exceeded; assemble final prompt context string/blocks.
  • Invariant: assembled context never exceeds ContextBudget.max_tokens; overflow always evicts working memories to long-term before truncating retrieval results; reserved tokens always held back for system prompt.
  • Failure modes: all records exceed budget → return single highest-salience record + truncation marker; LlmIface::count_tokens() unavailable → use character-based approximation (4 chars/token).

3.8 mem-consolidate — Background Consolidation Pipeline

  • Responsibilities: drain ConsolidationJob queue; run summarization (LlmIface::summarize()) on episodic clusters to produce semantic memories; run reflection (LlmIface::reflect()) on recent memory windows to produce higher-order insight records; merge near-duplicate records (cosine similarity > threshold); maintain links: Vec<MemoryId> graph edges; write new consolidated MemoryRecords back through mem-ingest.
  • Concurrency boundary: single background tokio::task; jobs are idempotent (re-runnable on restart); job state persisted in WAL (ConsolidationJob record type).
  • Failure modes: LlmIface call failure → job retried with exponential backoff (max 5 attempts); merge produces duplicate → dedup detects via content-hash, returns existing ID; reflection over corrupted cluster → skip cluster, emit telemetry warning.

3.9 mem-forget — Decay, Eviction, Tiering, Archival

  • Responsibilities: run periodic decay sweep: salience = salience * exp(-λ * Δt); evict records below salience threshold ε (configurable per namespace); enforce TTL (ttl: Option<Duration> field); move cold records to cold-tier (compressed storage or external object store); archive terminal records (write to archival WAL segment, remove from live index).
  • Invariant: every eviction writes a tombstone WAL entry with reason (decay, TTL, explicit forget, budget pressure) before index + store removal; no silent loss.
  • Tiering: hot (mmap + in-memory index), warm (mmap + index but lower priority), cold (compressed on-disk, excluded from default retrieval unless include_cold=true).
  • Failure modes: eviction of record with active links → write orphan-link warning to telemetry, proceed (dangling links are inert, not read); cold-tier write failure → retain in warm tier, emit metric.

3.10 mem-api — Public Agent-Facing Surface

  • Responsibilities: expose AgentMemory struct with remember(), recall(), forget(), reflect(), new_session(), set_namespace(); orchestrate calls across mem-ingest, mem-retrieve, mem-context, mem-consolidate, mem-forget; enforce namespace isolation (NsId plumbed through all calls); provide async Rust API.
  • Trust boundary: caller is the agent process; namespace isolation enforced at this layer; no cross-namespace data leakage.
  • Protocol boundary: pure Rust API (no HTTP, no gRPC at this layer); serialization only for persistence, not for IPC.

3.11 mem-telemetry — Observability Tap

  • Responsibilities: expose tracing spans and metrics counters/histograms for every hot path; per-crate instrumentation hooks (write latency, retrieval latency, index sizes, consolidation job throughput, eviction counts, embedding cache hit rate, WAL segment sizes); Prometheus-compatible export endpoint (optional feature flag).
  • Cross-cutting: taps all crates via tracing::instrument macros and metrics::counter! / metrics::histogram! calls; no crate depends on mem-telemetry (telemetry depends on crates, not vice versa).

3.12 Data Flow Summary

Step Source crate Destination crate Data
remember(obs) mem-api mem-ingest Observation
extract/embed mem-ingest LlmIface String → Vec<Fact>, Embedding
WAL write + store mem-ingest mem-store MemoryRecord
Index insert mem-store mem-index (MemoryId, Embedding, content)
recall(query) mem-api mem-retrieve Query
Parallel index search mem-retrieve mem-index ×4 Query → ranked MemoryId lists
RRF + MMR + rerank mem-retrieve → mem-context Vec<RetrievalResult>
Context pack mem-context → agent prompt context string
Consolidation mem-consolidate LlmIface → mem-ingest ConsolidationJob → new MemoryRecord
Decay / eviction mem-forget mem-store + mem-index tombstone WAL entries

4. System Constraints

4.1 Quantified Constraints Table

Constraint Target Notes
Throughput — ingest ≥ 500 remember()/s single-writer ingest channel; LlmIface calls batched
Throughput — retrieval ≥ 2,000 recall()/s concurrent read path; HNSW + BM25 in parallel
Latency — remember() p50 ≤ 10 ms excludes LlmIface round-trip (charged separately)
Latency — remember() p99 ≤ 50 ms includes WAL fsync (group-commit policy)
Latency — recall() p50 ≤ 5 ms full hybrid fusion; index in mmap hot path
Latency — recall() p99 ≤ 25 ms includes MMR pass; excludes rerank (optional, +50ms)
Latency — WAL fsync ≤ 2 ms p99 NVMe local disk; group-commit window ≤ 1 ms
Working memory capacity ≤ 8,192 tokens default configurable per ContextBudget; hard cap enforced
Long-term store size ≤ 10 GB per namespace before cold-tier migration; configurable
Max records per namespace ≤ 10 M records HNSW practical limit at M=16, ef_construction=200
Embedding dimension 768 or 1536 (configurable) fixed per namespace at creation; dim change = rebuild
HNSW M parameter 16 (default); range 8–64 higher M → better recall, more RAM
HNSW ef_construction 200 (default); range 50–500 higher → better index quality, slower insert
HNSW ef (search) 50 (default); tunable at query time recall@10 ≥ 0.95 at ef=50, M=16, 100K records
BM25 index size ≤ 2× corpus token count (bytes) inverted list compression with gap encoding
Consolidation throughput ≥ 100 jobs/s (non-LlmIface) background task; LlmIface calls serialized per job
Decay sweep period configurable; default 60 s incremental sweep; ≤ 5% CPU spike per sweep
Compaction frequency triggered at ≥ 20% dead records or ≥ 1 GB WAL background; no read-path stall
WAL segment size 64 MB default; configurable 16–512 MB segment rotation on size or age
mmap region ≤ 4 GB per record-store file 32-bit offset headroom; multi-file above threshold
Crash recovery time ≤ 30 s for 1 GB WAL sequential replay; parallel CRC check on segments
Startup time (cold) ≤ 5 s for 1 M records index load from snapshot; WAL delta replay
Shutdown time (graceful) ≤ 2 s drain ingest channel; flush WAL; close mmap
Max concurrency — readers 256 concurrent recall() calls bounded by tokio worker threads + HNSW snapshot
Fd budget ≤ 128 fds per store instance WAL segments + mmap files + LlmIface connections
RAM budget (index, hot) ≤ 2 GB for 1 M records at dim=768 HNSW graph ~1.4 GB; BM25 in-mem posting lists ~400 MB
CPU assumptions x86-64 (AVX2 for SIMD dot-product) ARM64 fallback via scalar path; no NUMA pinning (Phase 0)
IO amplification (compaction) ≤ 1.5× write amplification generation compaction merges 2 generations max
Availability target 99.9% uptime (embedded process) fault isolation: mem-store error returns MemError, agent continues
RTO (crash recovery) ≤ 30 s to fully operational WAL replay + index snapshot load
Failure budget ≤ 0.1% of remember() calls may return transient errors retried by caller; no data loss on retry

4.2 Latency Budget Breakdown (recall() p99 = 25 ms)

Stage Budget
Query embedding (cache hit) 0.5 ms
HNSW search (ef=50, 100K records) 3 ms
BM25 search 2 ms
Recency + importance scan 0.5 ms
RRF fusion 0.2 ms
MMR pass (top-50 candidates) 1 ms
Record fetch from mmap 1 ms
Context assembly 0.5 ms
Overhead (lock contention, serialization) ≤ 16.3 ms budget remaining (p99 tail)

4.3 Scaling Assumptions

  • Vertical: linear throughput scale with CPU cores (retrieval parallel; ingest single-writer).
  • Horizontal (Phase 0): not supported; single process, single node.
  • Horizontal (PhaseDeferred "16.x"): read replicas via snapshot export + WAL streaming.
  • Namespace isolation: each NsId has independent index + storage; no cross-namespace index contention.
  • Embedding provider scaling: LlmIface is async + connection-pooled; embedding latency is off the critical path for recall (uses cached embeddings).

4.4 Failure & Degradation Policy

Failure condition Behavior
WAL fsync failure remember() returns MemError::Io; no record persisted
mmap page fault (corruption) Process-level SIGBUS caught → MemError::StorageCorrupt; safe shutdown
LlmIface embedding timeout Record stored with embedding_ref: None; BM25 + recency retrieval only
LlmIface extraction timeout Record stored as raw content; facts/entities empty; consolidation retries
HNSW index corruption Detected at load (CRC fail) → rebuild from record store; recall falls back to BM25 during rebuild
Consolidation job failure Job retried ≤ 5 times with exponential backoff; failed job logged with full error chain
Disk full remember() returns MemError::StoreFull; read path unaffected
Eviction of linked record Tombstone written; links referencing evicted ID return MemError::NotFound on fetch
