6. Design Principles

6.1 P1 — Durability Before Acknowledgement

  • Statement: remember() returns Ok only after WAL entry is fsynced (or fdatasynced) per the configured flush policy; never buffer-and-ack.
  • Tradeoff: +latency per write; mitigated by group-commit batching (flush window ≤ 5 ms by default, tunable).
  • Operational: WAL flush latency is a P99 SLO target; alert on flush_latency_p99 > 20 ms.
  • Violation example: Returning Ok before fsync → on crash, agent believes memory is stored but WAL is empty → silent data loss, violates the core durability invariant.

6.2 P2 — Crash-Only / Replayability

  • Statement: All durable state transitions are written as structured WAL entries first; no in-place mutation of committed records; record store is fully rebuildable by WAL replay.
  • Tradeoff: Replay time grows with WAL size → compaction checkpoints bound recovery time; checkpoint interval is a tuning parameter.
  • Operational: Recovery time ≤ configurable target (default 30 s); mem-cli replay --dry-run verifies WAL integrity without side effects.
  • Violation example: Patching a record store page in-place without a WAL entry → partial write on crash → undetectable corruption; no way to reconstruct what changed.

6.3 P3 — Embedding–Record Atomicity

  • Statement: An embedding_ref written to a MemoryRecord and its corresponding vector in the ANN index are always consistent; neither dangles after compaction or delete.
  • Tradeoff: Compaction must update both record store and index atomically (two-phase: stage new index segment, swap, GC old); adds compaction complexity.
  • Operational: mem-cli fsck verifies embedding_ref→index consistency; telemetry counter index_dangling_refs_total must stay zero.
  • Violation example: Record store compaction removes a deleted record but leaves its HNSW node live → stale vectors pollute retrieval; score distribution drifts silently.

6.4 P4 — Retrieval Determinism

  • Statement: Given identical index state and Query, recall() must return identical ranked results. Tie-break: score DESC then MemoryId ASC.
  • Tradeoff: Determinism requires stable sort + deterministic float ops; hardware SIMD reordering must be accounted for via f32::total_cmp.
  • Operational: mem-bench regression tests record golden ranked sets; CI diff alerts on any rank change for a fixed query.
  • Violation example: Non-deterministic HashMap iteration order in fusion step → flapping CI, non-reproducible agent behavior, impossible to bisect retrieval quality regressions.

6.5 P5 — Bounded Resource Usage

  • Statement: Every sub-system declares explicit capacity bounds: WAL segment size, record store slab size, HNSW ef_construction, embedding cache entry count, consolidation job queue depth, working memory token budget. Exceeding a bound triggers backpressure or eviction, never unbounded growth.
  • Tradeoff: Bounds require upfront capacity planning; too-tight defaults cause unexpected evictions; document all bounds with justification.
  • Operational: Expose all bounds as config; alert on utilization > 80% of any bound; dashboard shows current vs max for each resource.
  • Violation example: Unbounded observation buffer in mem-ingest → spike in agent activity fills RAM → OOM kill → durability guarantee lost for buffered observations.

6.6 P6 — Observability by Default

  • Statement: Every cross-crate call, every retrieval component score, every background job, every eviction emits a structured telemetry event (span or metric) unconditionally. No opt-in tracing.
  • Tradeoff: +overhead per event (~100 ns for counter increment; ~500 ns for span creation); acceptable vs retrieval latency budget; sampling applied at export, not at instrumentation.
  • Operational: mem-telemetry must be initialized before any crate is used; test harness includes a no-op subscriber to prevent panic on missing subscriber.
  • Violation example: Omitting telemetry on the consolidation path → production consolidation failures are invisible; operator cannot distinguish slow LlmIface from consolidation job starvation.

6.7 P7 — Namespace Isolation

  • Statement: Every MemoryRecord, index entry, and WAL segment is scoped to a NsId; cross-namespace reads/writes are a hard error at the API boundary. Namespace isolation is enforced in mem-store and mem-index, not just in mem-api.
  • Tradeoff: Index-per-namespace multiplies index count → use a sharded design (namespace prefix in record key, namespace filter in index query, not separate index files) for high namespace counts.
  • Operational: Namespace creation is an explicit admin operation logged in WAL; mem-cli ns list shows per-namespace size/record count.
  • Violation example: Single global index without namespace filter → multi-tenant agent deployment leaks memories across users; GDPR/data-isolation violation; no per-user forget capability.

6.8 P8 — Explicit Provenance

  • Statement: Every MemoryRecord carries a fully populated Provenance { source, session, derived_from }. Derived (consolidated/reflected) memories chain back to source records via derived_from: Vec<MemoryId>. Provenance is immutable after write.
  • Tradeoff: Provenance chain can be long for multi-hop reflections; store only direct parents in derived_from; full lineage reconstructed by graph traversal.
  • Operational: mem-cli lineage <id> walks derived_from chain; telemetry records derivation depth histogram.
  • Violation example: Reflection memory without derived_from links → cannot audit what observations drove a high-salience belief; impossible to retract a belief if a source was erroneous.

6.9 P9 — Backpressure Everywhere

  • Statement: The write path (mem-ingest → mem-store) uses bounded channels; the consolidation job queue is bounded; the embedding request queue to LlmIface is bounded. When a queue is full, the caller blocks (sync context) or returns Err(MemError::Backpressure) (async context).
  • Tradeoff: Backpressure surfaces as latency to remember() callers; document latency SLO and queue depth defaults together.
  • Operational: Queue-depth metrics emitted per queue; alert on sustained > 80% utilization; distinguish slow-LlmIface from slow-store as root cause.
  • Violation example: Unbounded channel between ingest and store → fast producer / slow consumer → heap exhaustion; crash loses everything in channel.

6.10 P10 — Separation of Concerns: Memory vs. LLM

  • Statement: The LLM is accessed exclusively through LlmIface (summarize / extract / embed / rerank). No crate other than mem-embed and mem-ingest (and mem-consolidate via its job executor) calls LlmIface directly. LlmIface is always injected, never constructed internally.
  • Tradeoff: Adds an abstraction layer; integration tests can use a deterministic MockLlmIface without a real model.
  • Operational: Canary metric: LlmIface call rate per phase; spike indicates consolidation runaway.
  • Violation example: mem-retrieve calling LlmIface for reranking outside the declared rerank hook → unpredictable retrieval latency; rerank can be disabled but the call site is buried.

6.11 P11 — No Silent Loss

  • Statement: A memory is removed from the retrievable set only by: explicit forget() call, TTL expiry, or decay-driven eviction policy. Every removal is logged to WAL with reason code (explicit / ttl_expired / decay_evicted / archive_tiered). Compaction never silently drops records.
  • Tradeoff: Logging every eviction adds WAL write rate during mass eviction; use batched eviction WAL entries.
  • Operational: evictions_total counter by reason; mem-cli audit --evictions reconstructs full eviction history from WAL.
  • Violation example: Compaction drops low-salience records as a size optimization without WAL entry → operator cannot explain why a memory is gone; legal/audit liability in regulated deployments.

6.12 P12 — Fail Fast on Invariant Violation

  • Statement: Invariants (Section 7) that indicate logic bugs (not expected runtime errors) cause panic! with a structured message, not Err. Examples: NaN in Salience after normalization, dangling embedding_ref detected during read, monotonicity violation in MemoryId sequence.
  • Tradeoff: Panic crashes the process; acceptable because the WAL allows clean restart; a corrupted-but-running process is worse than a clean restart.
  • Operational: Panic messages include: invariant name, offending value, MemoryId, namespace; crash log collector aggregates these.
  • Violation example: Silently clamping a NaN Salience to 0.0 → corrupted record participates in retrieval with wrong score; silent data corruption harder to diagnose than an explicit crash.

6.13 P13 — Schema Stability and Forwards Compatibility

  • Statement: WAL entry format, MemoryRecord on-disk layout, and HNSW graph serialization each include a format version header. Readers must handle current and all prior versions; writers always emit the current version. Breaking changes require a WAL segment boundary + migration.
  • Tradeoff: Version negotiation adds code surface; enumerate format versions in a WalVersion enum, keep migration functions in mem-store.
  • Operational: mem-cli inspect-wal prints version histogram; alert if mixed versions span > 1 major.
  • Violation example: Silently changing MemoryRecord binary layout → WAL replay after upgrade reads garbage fields → corrupted salience scores → retrieval nonsense; undetectable without version header.

6.14 P14 — Test Fidelity: Mock LlmIface Is the Norm

  • Statement: All unit and integration tests use MockLlmIface (deterministic, seed-controlled). Real LLM calls are confined to a separate e2e test suite gated by --feature live-llm. Mock must implement the full LlmIface contract including latency simulation and error injection.
  • Tradeoff: Mock drift from real LLM behavior → false confidence; mock is validated against real responses in a monthly golden-set test.
  • Violation example: Test suite calling real LLM → CI is non-deterministic, expensive, rate-limited; test failures conflate code bugs with API outages.

6.15 P15 — Monotonic Time and Sortable IDs

  • Statement: MemoryId(Ulid) and created_at are always set at ingest time using a monotonic clock source; they are immutable after write. as_of time-travel queries rely on created_at monotonicity for correctness. Clock rollback (e.g., NTP correction) must be handled by ULID monotonic generation (increment random bits on same millisecond).
  • Tradeoff: ULID monotonic mode requires per-process state; inter-process monotonicity is millisecond-granular only.
  • Operational: mem-telemetry records clock skew metric; alert on backward jumps > 1 s.
  • Violation example: Using SystemTime::now() without monotonic guard → two records in the same ms get same timestamp prefix → sort instability → as-of query returns inconsistent sets.

7. System Invariants

7.1 I1 — Durability Invariant

  • Statement: If remember() returns Ok(MemoryId), the corresponding WAL entry is fsync'd (or fdatasync'd) to durable media and is recoverable after any single-node crash.
  • Why: Agents rely on Ok as a durable-write confirmation; silent loss breaks agent reasoning chains.
  • Enforced in: mem-store WAL writer; flush policy gate before Ok propagates to mem-api.
  • Detection: Post-crash WAL replay in integration test with kill-9 injection; wal_flush_failures_total counter.
  • Telemetry: wal_flush_latency_us histogram; wal_flush_failures_total; wal_bytes_written_total.
  • Recovery: Unsynced entries never acked → no phantom records; replay from last synced entry is safe idempotent.
  • Debug: mem-cli replay --verify walks WAL, checks each entry's CRC32, reports gaps; compare with record-store state.

7.2 I2 — No-Silent-Loss Invariant

  • Statement: A MemoryRecord transitions out of the retrievable set only via explicit forget(), TTL expiry, or decay eviction; every transition is written to WAL with a typed reason code before the record is removed.
  • Why: Unexplained memory disappearance corrupts agent reasoning; required for auditable forget in regulated systems.
  • Enforced in: mem-forget eviction paths; mem-store delete entry; WAL entry type EvictRecord { id, reason, ts }.
  • Detection: mem-cli audit --evictions <namespace> replays WAL and outputs full eviction log; unit test verifies every delete path emits WAL entry before removing.
  • Telemetry: evictions_total{reason} counter; evictions_batch_size histogram.
  • Recovery: WAL is the authoritative eviction log; record store reconciles on replay.
  • Debug: If a record is missing unexpectedly, mem-cli lineage <id> + eviction log identifies the reason and timestamp.

7.3 I3 — Embedding–Record Consistency Invariant

  • Statement: For every retrievable MemoryRecord, its embedding_ref resolves to a valid vector in the ANN index. No dangling embedding_refs; no orphan index nodes without a corresponding live record.
  • Why: Dangling refs return stale vectors in retrieval; orphan nodes inflate recall and corrupt score distributions.
  • Enforced in: mem-store + mem-index compaction runs atomically (stage new index segment, swap, GC old within a single compaction transaction); delete path removes from both.
  • Detection: mem-cli fsck performs full consistency scan; index_dangling_refs_total gauge; index_orphan_nodes_total gauge — both must be zero.
  • Telemetry: compaction_embedding_mismatches_total; fsck_duration_ms.
  • Recovery: mem-cli fsck --repair re-indexes records missing from ANN; removes orphan nodes; emits repair WAL entries.
  • Debug: Discrepancy between record-store count and index node count → trigger fsck; diff output identifies specific MemoryIds affected.

7.4 I4 — Retrieval Determinism Invariant

  • Statement: For fixed index state S and Query Q, recall(Q) always returns identical ranked list with identical scores. Tie-break: score DESC, id ASC using f32::total_cmp for NaN-safe total order.
  • Why: Non-determinism makes quality regressions impossible to bisect; A/B testing of retrieval changes requires a stable baseline.
  • Enforced in: mem-retrieve fusion pipeline; mem-index HNSW search (fixed ef_search, fixed entry point seed); BM25 scorer (deterministic IDF computation).
  • Detection: mem-bench golden-set test: record ranked list for N queries at a fixed snapshot; assert identical on re-run.
  • Telemetry: retrieval_nondeterminism_events_total (incremented if golden-set test diverges in CI).
  • Recovery: Not applicable — non-determinism is a code bug; fix source of randomness (HashMap ordering → BTreeMap; float reordering → total_cmp).
  • Debug: Bisect by disabling individual retrieval components (vec / bm25 / recency / importance) until non-determinism disappears; check HashMap → BTreeMap in fusion accumulators.

7.5 I5 — Context-Budget Invariant

  • Statement: The token count of the assembled context returned by mem-context is always ≤ ContextBudget.max_tokens. Overflow triggers working-memory eviction (lowest priority first) to long-term store before assembly completes.
  • Why: Exceeding the LLM context window causes truncation or API errors at the agent level; the memory subsystem must never hand the agent an oversized context.
  • Enforced in: mem-context assembler token-counting loop; eviction triggered inline before returning context.
  • Detection: context_overflow_events_total counter; assert in assembler that returned token count ≤ budget.
  • Telemetry: context_assembled_tokens histogram; context_evictions_total; context_budget_utilization gauge.
  • Recovery: On overflow, evict lowest-salience working memories to episodic store, log eviction, retry packing.
  • Debug: context_assembled_tokens p99 approaching max_tokens → increase budget or reduce k in recall; distinguish "budget too small" from "retrieval returns too much".

7.6 I6 — Monotonic ID / Time Invariant

  • Statement: MemoryId(Ulid) values are monotonically non-decreasing within a process. created_at timestamps are monotonically non-decreasing. Neither is modified after initial write. as_of queries: results contain only records with created_at ≤ as_of.
  • Why: ULID sort order is used for recency index and WAL ordering; as-of time-travel correctness depends on monotonic created_at.
  • Enforced in: mem-core ULID generator (monotonic mode); mem-store WAL writer (asserts monotonicity before append); as-of query filter in mem-index recency index.
  • Detection: WAL reader checks ULID sequence on replay; monotonicity violation → WAL corruption error; monotonicity_violations_total counter.
  • Telemetry: ulid_generation_collisions_total (same-millisecond increments); clock_skew_ms gauge.
  • Recovery: Monotonicity violation in WAL replay → abort replay, report corruption range, require manual inspection; cannot silently skip.
  • Debug: mem-cli inspect-wal --check-monotonicity reports first violation; correlate with system clock NTP logs.

7.7 I7 — Namespace Isolation Invariant

  • Statement: A read or write operation with NsId(A) never returns, modifies, or evicts records belonging to NsId(B) where A ≠ B. Enforced at storage and index layers, not only at API layer.
  • Why: Multi-tenant deployments require hard data isolation; API-layer-only enforcement is trivially bypassed by direct store access.
  • Enforced in: mem-store key format includes NsId prefix; mem-index query always filters by NsId; mem-api validates NsId on every call.
  • Detection: Integration test: write record in NsId(A); assert recall in NsId(B) returns empty; cross_namespace_access_attempts_total counter (should always be zero after enforcement; non-zero = bug).
  • Telemetry: cross_namespace_access_attempts_total; namespace-scoped records_total gauge.
  • Recovery: Cross-namespace access is a code bug → panic in debug builds; return MemError::NamespaceViolation in release builds + alert.
  • Debug: cross_namespace_access_attempts_total > 0 → grep for NsId construction without caller-supplied value; check namespace propagation through async tasks.

7.8 I8 — WAL Append-Only Invariant

  • Statement: The WAL is strictly append-only. No WAL entry is ever modified or deleted before a compaction checkpoint. Compaction creates a new checkpoint file; old WAL segments are deleted only after the checkpoint is fsync'd and verified.
  • Why: Any in-place WAL modification can corrupt the recovery log; old segments must survive until new checkpoint is proven valid.
  • Enforced in: mem-store WAL writer (O_APPEND open flag; no seek-and-write); compaction: fsync checkpoint → verify → unlink old segments.
  • Detection: WAL writer opens file with O_APPEND | O_CREAT; mem-cli inspect-wal detects non-monotonic offsets.
  • Telemetry: wal_segment_count; wal_bytes_reclaimable (bytes in pre-checkpoint segments).
  • Recovery: If checkpoint verification fails, retain old WAL segments; retry compaction; never proceed to segment deletion without verified checkpoint.
  • Debug: Segment count growing without compaction → check compaction job is running; check wal_bytes_reclaimable trend.

7.9 I9 — Salience Range Invariant

  • Statement: Salience(f32) is always in [0.0, 1.0] and is never NaN or Inf. Violated values cause panic! in debug and MemError::InvalidSalience in release after logging.
  • Why: Salience drives ranking and eviction priority; out-of-range values corrupt sort orders and eviction decisions silently.
  • Enforced in: Salience::new(f32) -> Result<Salience, MemError> constructor validates range; LlmIface response parsing clamps before constructing Salience.
  • Detection: salience_out_of_range_total counter; mem-cli fsck --check-salience scans record store.
  • Telemetry: salience_distribution histogram (p5/p50/p95); salience_out_of_range_total.
  • Recovery: On read, out-of-range Salience triggers fsck on the affected record; operator decides to recompute or tombstone.
  • Debug: Out-of-range Salience almost always from LlmIface returning un-normalized importance scores; add clamp + log in LlmIface adapter.

7.10 I10 — Index–Store Record Count Invariant

  • Statement: For each (NsId, MemoryKind) partition: the count of live records in the record store equals the count of indexed entries across all indices (ANN + BM25 + recency + importance). Discrepancy > 0 indicates a consistency bug.
  • Why: Over-indexing returns phantom results; under-indexing causes retrieval misses on valid memories.
  • Enforced in: Ingest and delete are always two-phase: WAL entry → store write → index write (all in a logical transaction); compaction syncs counts.
  • Detection: mem-cli fsck --count-check computes per-partition counts and diffs; index_count_mismatch_total gauge.
  • Telemetry: store_record_count{ns, kind} gauge; index_entry_count{ns, kind, index_type} gauge; index_count_mismatch_total.
  • Recovery: mem-cli fsck --repair re-indexes records missing from indices; tombstones index entries without backing records.
  • Debug: Mismatch after crash → likely partial index write before WAL-based index recovery; check index recovery path in mem-index startup.

7.11 I11 — TTL Expiry Invariant

  • Statement: A MemoryRecord with ttl: Some(d) is not returned by recall() after created_at + d. Expired records are tombstoned (eviction WAL entry) and excluded from all index queries. Tombstoning is lazy (at query time) or eager (TTL sweep), but never retroactively absent from WAL.
  • Why: TTL is a user-visible contract; returning expired memories breaks agent trust in temporal accuracy.
  • Enforced in: mem-index query filter checks created_at + ttl ≤ now; mem-forget TTL sweep marks expired records.
  • Detection: Unit test: create record with ttl=1s; sleep 2s; assert recall returns empty; ttl_expired_total counter.
  • Telemetry: ttl_expired_total; ttl_sweep_duration_ms; ttl_pending_count gauge.
  • Recovery: If TTL sweep falls behind, query-time filter is the safety net; alert on ttl_pending_count > threshold.
  • Debug: Expired records appearing in results → check query-time TTL filter is applied before score fusion, not after.

7.12 I12 — Consolidation Idempotency Invariant

  • Statement: Re-running a ConsolidationJob with the same input MemoryIds and the same LlmIface response produces the same output MemoryRecord (deterministic by job content, idempotent by dedup on derived_from set). Re-running does not create duplicate reflection memories.
  • Why: Consolidation jobs may be retried after failure; duplicates inflate the memory store and corrupt salience distributions.
  • Enforced in: mem-consolidate dedup check: before writing reflection record, query for existing records with matching derived_from set; skip if found.
  • Detection: Unit test: run consolidation job twice with MockLlmIface; assert record count unchanged on second run.
  • Telemetry: consolidation_dedup_hits_total; consolidation_jobs_total{status}.
  • Recovery: If duplicate reflection records exist (detected by fsck), tombstone duplicates (keep highest-salience); log repair action.
  • Debug: Rising consolidation_dedup_hits_total with no new memories → consolidation job is being retried excessively; check job queue and LlmIface error rate.

8. Domain Model — AI Agent Memory Lifecycle & Retrieval

8.1 Entity Catalogue

Entity Identity Mutable Fields Immutable Fields
MemoryRecord MemoryId(Ulid) salience, last_access, access_count, links, ttl id, kind, content, embedding_ref, created_at, provenance, namespace
Embedding EmbeddingId(Ulid) — vector: Vec<f32>, dim: u32, model_id: str, created_at
NsId u64 (hash of namespace string) policy: NsPolicy name, created_at
ConsolidationJob JobId(Ulid) state, retries, result kind, source_ids, scheduled_at
DecayEntry composite (NsId, MemoryId) score, tier, evicted_at created_at
SessionCtx SessionId(Ulid) working_memory: Vec<MemoryId>, budget_used ns, opened_at

Ownership: MemoryRecord owns provenance; Embedding is referenced by EmbeddingId — embedding store is separate from record store; records never embed vector bytes inline.

8.2 MemoryRecord State Machine

Show the original text diagram
  [ Observed ]
       |  mem-ingest: extract + dedup check
       v
  [ Pending ] ──dup detected──> [ Deduplicated ] (existing record's access_count++)
       |  embed + store
       v
  [ Active ] ──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
       |                                                        |
  consolidation tick                                    explicit forget()
       |                                                        |
       v                                                        v
  [ Consolidated ] (original archived or replaced)     [ Tombstoned ]
       |                                                        |
  decay score < eviction_threshold                      WAL TOMBSTONE written
       |                                                        |
       v                                                        v
  [ Warm ]  ──further decay──> [ Cold ] ──TTL/policy──> [ Archived/Deleted ]

State transitions — invariant: transitions are WAL-logged before index update.

Transition Trigger WAL Entry Index Op
Observed → Pending remember() call OP_BEGIN_INGEST none
Pending → Active embed + fsync OP_RECORD_COMMITTED insert all indices
Active → Consolidated consolidation job OP_CONSOLIDATED(src_ids, new_id) replace src with new
Active → Tombstoned forget() API OP_TOMBSTONE(id, reason, ts) remove all indices
Active → Warm decay score drop OP_TIER_CHANGE(id, Hot→Warm) importance index update
Warm → Cold further decay OP_TIER_CHANGE(id, Warm→Cold) evict from hot cache
Cold → Archived TTL/policy OP_ARCHIVE(id, dest_path) remove live indices

8.3 Write Path Sequence (remember())

Show the original text diagram
Agent
  │ remember(obs: Observation, ns: NsId, session: SessionId)
  ▼
mem-api ──validates ns, session──> mem-ingest
  │
  ├─ LlmIface::extract_facts(obs.content) ──> Vec<Fact>
  ├─ dedup_check(content_hash, ns) vs record store
  │     hit  ──> bump access_count, return existing MemoryId (no new record)
  │     miss ──> continue
  ├─ chunker::split(content, chunk_cfg) ──> Vec<Chunk>
  ├─ mem-embed::embed_chunks(chunks) ──> Vec<(ChunkId, Embedding)>
  │     cache hit: skip LlmIface call; cache miss: LlmIface::embed()
  ├─ build MemoryRecord { kind, content, embedding_ref, salience=initial_salience(), ... }
  ├─ mem-store::append_wal(OP_BEGIN_INGEST)
  ├─ mem-store::write_record(record)
  ├─ mem-store::fsync() [per SyncPolicy]
  ├─ mem-index::insert(vec_index, bm25_index, recency_index, importance_index)
  ├─ mem-store::append_wal(OP_RECORD_COMMITTED)
  └─ return Ok(MemoryId)

Failure paths:
  embed failure        ──> OP_INGEST_ABORTED, record not committed, no index mutation
  fsync failure        ──> error propagated, partial WAL entry detected on next recovery
  index insert failure ──> OP_INDEX_PARTIAL logged; repair job scheduled

Timing contract: WAL fsync before returning Ok (strict durability); or per SyncPolicy::Batched(interval) (relaxed, documented trade-off).

8.4 Read Path Sequence (recall())

Show the original text diagram
Agent
  │ recall(query: Query, ns: NsId)
  ▼
mem-api ──validates budget, ns──> mem-retrieve
  │
  ├─ [query.embedding absent] ──> mem-embed::embed_query(query.text) ──> Embedding
  │
  ├─ PARALLEL branch (4 lanes):
  │   ├─ vec_index::search(embedding, k*4)   ──> Vec<(MemoryId, f32)>  [cosine sim]
  │   ├─ bm25_index::search(tokens, k*4)     ──> Vec<(MemoryId, f32)>  [BM25 score]
  │   ├─ recency_index::top_k(k*4)           ──> Vec<(MemoryId, f32)>  [exp decay]
  │   └─ importance_index::top_k(k*4)        ──> Vec<(MemoryId, f32)>  [salience]
  │
  ├─ RRF_fuse(lists, recency_bias, importance_bias) ──> Vec<(MemoryId, f32)>
  ├─ mem-store::batch_fetch(ids)             ──> Vec<MemoryRecord>
  ├─ apply kind_filter, MetaFilter, as_of
  ├─ MMR_rerank(candidates, query_embedding, lambda=0.5) ──> top-k diverse set
  ├─ [rerank_hook present] ──> LlmIface::rerank(query, candidates) ──> reordered
  ├─ update last_access + access_count (async write-back, not on critical path)
  └─ return Vec<RetrievalResult { record, score, components { vec, bm25, recency, importance } }>

as_of query: filters records where created_at > as_of before index lookup (index is time-stamped).

8.5 RRF Fusion Formula

RRF_score(d) = Σ_i  w_i / (k_rrf + rank_i(d))

where:
  i ∈ { vec, bm25, recency, importance }
  w_vec         = 1.0 - recency_bias - importance_bias          (≥ 0)
  w_bm25        = 1.0 - recency_bias - importance_bias          (shared with vec, normalized)
  w_recency     = recency_bias  (Query.recency_bias ∈ [0,1])
  w_importance  = importance_bias
  k_rrf         = 60 (default, configurable)

Tie-break: equal RRF score → lower MemoryId (Ulid, lexicographic = chronological).
Invariant: deterministic given fixed index state + query.

8.6 MMR Diversity Reranking

MMR(query q, candidate set S, selected set R, lambda λ):
  score(d) = λ · sim(d, q) - (1-λ) · max_{r∈R} sim(d, r)
  greedily select d* = argmax score(d) from S \ R; add to R; repeat k times.

λ = 0 → max diversity; λ = 1 → pure relevance.
Default λ = 0.5 (configurable per Query).
sim = cosine similarity on Embedding vectors.
Complexity: O(k · |S| · D) where D = embedding dim; acceptable for k ≤ 50, |S| ≤ 200.

8.7 Working Memory & Context Assembly

SessionCtx.working_memory = circular buffer, capacity = cfg.working_mem_cap (default 32 MemoryIds)
ContextBudget { max_tokens: usize, reserved: usize }  → available = max_tokens - reserved

Assembly algorithm (mem-context):
  1. Rank working memories by (salience DESC, last_access DESC).
  2. Greedily pack records until token count would exceed (max_tokens - reserved).
  3. Overflow: evict lowest-priority working memories to long-term (episodic) store.
     - Eviction = append OP_EVICT_TO_LONGTERM(id) to WAL; remove from working_memory buffer.
  4. Append retrieved long-term memories (from recall()) similarly.
  5. Return ContextPacket { items: Vec<MemoryRecord>, tokens_used }.

Invariant: tokens_used ≤ max_tokens always; overflow raises ContextOverflowError not silent truncation.
Edge case: single record > available tokens → error MemError::RecordExceedsBudget(id).

8.8 Consolidation Pipeline

ConsolidationJob kinds:
  Summarize   — group episodic memories by time window / topic → one SemanticMemory
  Reflect     — LlmIface::reflect(memories) → higher-order insight → new SemanticMemory
  Merge       — near-duplicate records (cosine sim > merge_threshold) → single record
  LinkGraph   — extract entity co-occurrence → update MemoryRecord.links

Scheduling: background tokio task, interval-driven; also triggered when
  episodic_count(ns) > cfg.consolidation_trigger_count.

Execution sequence:
  1. ConsolidationScheduler picks job from queue (priority: Merge > Summarize > Reflect).
  2. Lock source records (optimistic: read-only; no write lock on record store).
  3. LlmIface call (summarize / reflect / embed result).
  4. Write new MemoryRecord (kind=Semantic or Procedural).
  5. WAL: OP_CONSOLIDATED(source_ids: Vec<MemoryId>, new_id: MemoryId).
  6. Update indices: insert new, remove/mark source records (tombstone or downgrade salience).
  7. Update MemoryRecord.links on related records.
  8. Release.

Failure modes:
  LlmIface timeout   → job retried with exponential backoff (max 3 retries); sources untouched.
  Partial write      → WAL replay detects OP_BEGIN_CONSOLIDATE without matching OP_CONSOLIDATED → re-run.
  Duplicate reflect  → content_hash dedup prevents identical insight from being stored twice.

ConsolidationJob state machine:

State → Next Condition
Queued Running scheduler picks up
Running Done LlmIface + WAL commit succeed
Running Failed LlmIface error or WAL error
Failed Queued retry_count < max_retries
Failed Abandoned retry_count ≥ max_retries; alert emitted
Done — terminal

8.9 Decay & Forgetting Model

decay_score(record, t_now) =
    w_r · recency(record, t_now)
  + w_s · record.salience.0
  + w_a · access_frequency(record)

recency(r, t) = exp(- λ_r · (t - r.last_access).as_secs_f64() / 86400.0)
  λ_r default = 0.1 (half-life ≈ 7 days for λ=0.1)
access_frequency(r) = min(1.0, r.access_count as f32 / cfg.access_saturation)

w_r + w_s + w_a = 1.0 (configurable; defaults 0.4 / 0.4 / 0.2)

Tiering thresholds (configurable per NsId):
  score > hot_threshold   → Hot (in-memory index + mmap)
  warm_threshold < score ≤ hot_threshold → Warm (mmap only)
  cold_threshold < score ≤ warm_threshold → Cold (file, not mmap-pinned)
  score ≤ cold_threshold  → eviction candidate

Eviction policy (mem-forget):
  1. Collect Cold records where score ≤ cold_threshold OR ttl expired.
  2. Sort by score ASC (lowest first).
  3. Evict until free_target_bytes freed or eviction_list exhausted.
  4. WAL: OP_EVICT(id, reason: {decay|ttl|capacity}, score, ts).
  5. Remove from all live indices.
  6. Write to archive store (optional, per NsPolicy.archive_on_evict).

Invariant: every eviction logged with reason; no silent loss.
TTL: if record.ttl = Some(d) and (t_now - created_at) > d → forced eviction regardless of score.

8.10 WAL Record Layout

WAL segment file: fixed-path  data/wal/{ns_hex}/{seg_id:08}.wal
Header per segment:
  magic:      [u8; 4]  = b"MWAL"
  version:    u16
  segment_id: u64
  created_at: i64 (Unix ns)

Per-entry framing (little-endian):
  entry_len:  u32       (total bytes of payload + crc)
  op_type:    u8        (enum discriminant; see table below)
  payload:    [u8]      (bincode-encoded op-specific struct)
  crc32c:     u32       (covers op_type + payload)

Op codes:
  0x01  OP_BEGIN_INGEST       { ingest_id: Ulid, ns: NsId, session: SessionId }
  0x02  OP_RECORD_COMMITTED   { id: MemoryId, embedding_ref: EmbeddingId, kind: MemoryKind }
  0x03  OP_INGEST_ABORTED     { ingest_id: Ulid, reason: String }
  0x10  OP_TOMBSTONE          { id: MemoryId, reason: ForgetReason, ts: Timestamp }
  0x11  OP_TIER_CHANGE        { id: MemoryId, from: Tier, to: Tier, ts: Timestamp }
  0x12  OP_EVICT              { id: MemoryId, reason: EvictReason, score: f32, ts: Timestamp }
  0x13  OP_ARCHIVE            { id: MemoryId, dest: PathBuf }
  0x20  OP_BEGIN_CONSOLIDATE  { job_id: JobId, source_ids: Vec<MemoryId> }
  0x21  OP_CONSOLIDATED       { job_id: JobId, new_id: MemoryId }
  0x22  OP_CONSOLIDATE_FAIL   { job_id: JobId, reason: String }
  0x30  OP_EVICT_TO_LONGTERM  { id: MemoryId, session: SessionId }
  0x40  OP_SNAPSHOT           { snapshot_id: Ulid, lsn: u64, ts: Timestamp }
  0x41  OP_COMPACTION_DONE    { new_segment: u64, obsolete_segments: Vec<u64> }
  0xFF  OP_CHECKPOINT         { lsn: u64 }

Recovery protocol:
  1. Find last OP_CHECKPOINT; start replay from that LSN.
  2. For each entry: verify CRC32c; skip corrupt entry (log warning + metrics bump).
  3. Apply in order; ignore entries for already-committed records (idempotent).
  4. Any OP_BEGIN_* without matching OP_*_COMMITTED/FAIL → abort that operation.
  5. Rebuild indices from replayed committed records.

8.11 Record Store Layout (mmap)

data/store/{ns_hex}/records.dat   — append-only fixed-slot or variable-length records
data/store/{ns_hex}/embeddings.dat — embedding vectors, aligned to 64 bytes (SIMD)
data/store/{ns_hex}/index/hnsw.bin — HNSW graph serialized
data/store/{ns_hex}/index/bm25.bin — inverted index
data/store/{ns_hex}/index/recency.idx — sorted ring of (created_at, MemoryId)
data/store/{ns_hex}/index/importance.idx — sorted heap of (salience, MemoryId)
data/snapshots/{snap_id}.snap — snapshot (records.dat + index files, linked to WAL LSN)

Record slot in records.dat:
  slot_header: { id: [u8;16], flags: u8, payload_len: u32 }
  payload:     bincode(MemoryRecord) — variable length
  padding:     align to 8 bytes

Flags:
  0x01 = ACTIVE
  0x02 = TOMBSTONED
  0x04 = CONSOLIDATED_SOURCE (kept for provenance, excluded from retrieval)
  0x08 = ARCHIVED

mmap strategy: records.dat + embeddings.dat mapped read-only for retrieval; writes go via pwrite().
Compaction: new file written with only ACTIVE records; atomic rename; WAL OP_COMPACTION_DONE.

8.12 HNSW Index Semantics

Parameters (configurable):
  M       = 16   (max neighbours per node per layer)
  ef_c    = 200  (construction ef)
  ef_s    = 50   (search ef; tunable at query time)
  dim     = embedding dimension (must match model; validated at insert)

Distance: cosine (pre-normalize vectors to unit length → inner product = cosine).
Insert: add_node(embedding_ref: EmbeddingId, vec: &[f32]) → O(log N) amortized.
Search: search_knn(query_vec: &[f32], k: usize, ef: usize) → Vec<(EmbeddingId, f32)>.
Delete: lazy-tombstone in HNSW (set node flag DELETED); periodic background rebuild removes dead nodes.
  Invariant: deleted nodes never returned in search results (filter applied post-search).
Persistence: serialize to hnsw.bin on checkpoint; load on startup; rebuild from embeddings.dat if corrupt.
Concurrent reads: allowed (shared reader lock); writes serialized (exclusive writer lock on HNSW struct).

Edge case: dim mismatch on insert → MemError::DimMismatch { expected, got }.
Edge case: N = 0 (empty index) → search returns empty Vec (not error).

8.13 BM25 Inverted Index Semantics

Tokenizer: Unicode word boundary split + lowercase + stop-word filter (configurable list).
Stemming: optional (Porter stemmer); disabled by default (reproducibility).
Index entry: term → posting_list: [(MemoryId, tf: u32, field_len: u16)]
IDF: ln(1 + (N - df + 0.5) / (df + 0.5))   (Robertson IDF)
TF-norm: (tf · (k1+1)) / (tf + k1 · (1 - b + b · dl/avgdl))   k1=1.2, b=0.75
Score(q, d) = Σ_{t∈q} IDF(t) · TF_norm(t, d)

Updates: insert on commit; delete marks posting entries as tombstoned; compaction removes them.
Persistence: serialized to bm25.bin.
Edge case: query terms not in any document → score 0 for all; RRF rank = ∞ (treated as absent from list).
Pitfall: very short documents inflate TF-norm; field_len tracked per record.

8.14 Namespace Isolation

NsId = hash64(namespace_string)
Each namespace owns:
  - Separate WAL directory: data/wal/{ns_hex}/
  - Separate record store: data/store/{ns_hex}/
  - Separate index files
  - NsPolicy { sync_policy, archive_on_evict, consolidation_enabled, decay_params, max_records }

Cross-namespace: records are never retrievable across namespaces by default.
  Exception: explicit cross-ns link if NsPolicy.allow_cross_ns_links = true.
Isolation invariant: a Query with ns=A never returns records from ns=B.
  Enforcement: all index lookups are ns-scoped; record fetch validates ns field.

Pitfall: ns hash collision (u64) → two namespaces map to same store path.
  Mitigation: log warning + refuse second ns creation if path already claimed by different name.

8.15 Provenance & Link Graph

Provenance { source: SourceId, session: SessionId, derived_from: Vec<MemoryId> }
  source: enum { Agent(AgentId), User, System, Consolidation(JobId) }
  derived_from: populated by consolidation (new record points to source records).

MemoryRecord.links: Vec<MemoryId> — bidirectional association maintained by mem-consolidate.
Link graph traversal: BFS from seed MemoryId, depth-limited (default depth=2).
  Used by: context assembly (pull related memories), reflect job (find clusters).

Invariant: derived_from entries reference valid MemoryIds (not tombstoned at consolidation time;
  subsequent tombstone of a source is allowed — link becomes "stale ref", tracked in link_graph audit).
Pitfall: circular links (A → B → A) possible via consolidation chains; traversal must track visited set.

8.16 Worked Example — Full Lifecycle

t=0: agent calls remember("User prefers dark mode", ns="user:alice", session=S1)
  → ingest: extract fact "preference: dark_mode=true"
  → dedup: content_hash not seen → proceed
  → embed: EmbeddingId=E1, dim=1536
  → MemoryRecord { id=M1, kind=Semantic, content="...", embedding_ref=E1, salience=0.7, ... }
  → WAL: OP_BEGIN_INGEST | OP_RECORD_COMMITTED(M1, E1)
  → indices: HNSW insert(E1, vec), BM25 index tokens, recency ring, importance heap
  → Ok(M1)

t=1: agent calls recall(Query { text="UI preferences", k=5 }, ns="user:alice")
  → embed query → E_q
  → vec search(E_q, 20) → [(M1, 0.92), ...]
  → bm25 search("ui preferences") → [(M1, 3.1), ...]
  → recency top-20 → [M1, ...]
  → importance top-20 → [M1, ...]
  → RRF fuse → M1 score=0.89
  → MMR rerank (k=5, λ=0.5) → [M1, ...]
  → return [RetrievalResult { record=M1, score=0.89, components={vec:0.92,bm25:0.78,recency:0.95,importance:0.7} }]

t=48h: decay tick
  → decay_score(M1, t_now) = 0.4·exp(-0.1·2) + 0.4·0.7 + 0.2·0.1 ≈ 0.4·0.82 + 0.28 + 0.02 = 0.63
  → above warm_threshold(0.4) → remains Active/Hot

t=30d: decay_score(M1) drops below warm_threshold → Warm tier; WAL OP_TIER_CHANGE

t=90d: TTL expires (if ttl=90d) → WAL OP_EVICT(M1, reason=ttl) → removed from indices

8.17 Edge Cases & Malformed Input Table

Scenario Behaviour Invariant enforced
remember() with empty content MemError::EmptyContent no WAL entry written
remember() embedding dim mismatch MemError::DimMismatch record not committed
recall() k=0 returns empty Vec (not error) determinism
recall() with as_of in future returns full set (no filtering) monotonic ids
Duplicate content same ns returns existing MemoryId; access_count++ dedup invariant
WAL CRC mismatch on recovery skip entry, log warning, metrics wal_corrupt_entries no silent data loss (entry skipped not silently accepted)
HNSW rebuild needed (corrupt hnsw.bin) rebuild from embeddings.dat; slow but safe index-store consistency
ConsolidationJob LlmIface timeout retry ≤3; sources unchanged; job Abandoned after max no partial consolidation
Cross-ns recall attempt MemError::NamespaceMismatch isolation invariant
ContextBudget overflow (single record > budget) MemError::RecordExceedsBudget(id) budget invariant
Circular link traversal visited-set BFS terminates no infinite loop
HNSW insert with N at capacity resize / new segment; O(1) amortized no data loss

8.18 Failure-Path Sequence: Crash During remember()

Show the original text diagram
Scenario: process crashes after WAL fsync of OP_BEGIN_INGEST but before OP_RECORD_COMMITTED.

Recovery:
  1. WAL replay finds OP_BEGIN_INGEST(ingest_id=I1) with no matching OP_RECORD_COMMITTED.
  2. Classify as INCOMPLETE_INGEST.
  3. Check record store: if record with embedding_ref exists (partial write) → tombstone it.
  4. Check embedding store: if E1 exists orphaned → mark for GC.
  5. Log warning: "Incomplete ingest I1 during recovery; aborted."
  6. Increment metric: recovery_aborted_ingests_total.
  7. System resumes clean; no half-committed record visible to retrieval.

Key: no index was mutated before OP_RECORD_COMMITTED → indices remain consistent.

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust edition 2021, MSRV 1.78 Python, Go, C++ Storage-engine correctness (WAL, mmap, crash safety); ownership model prevents data races in concurrent retrieval + consolidation; no GC pauses; safe FFI for embedding/rerank backends Python wins when iteration velocity outweighs correctness (early-stage research prototype, ≤1k memories, no durability requirement). Go wins when a small team maintains a microservice that uses an existing Postgres-backed store (no custom WAL needed, GC latency acceptable). C++ wins when the team needs to embed in an existing C++ agent runtime with shared-memory zero-copy to GPU.
ADR-02 Build system Cargo workspaces (13 crates) Bazel, Buck, CMake Native Rust toolchain; workspace unifies deps; cargo build --release for production Bazel wins when this project is one module among hundreds in a large monorepo requiring hermetic, cross-language (Rust + Python + Proto) builds, remote cache, and deterministic sandboxed CI. CMake wins if the final artifact must be linked into a C/C++ host system with system-level packaging requirements.
ADR-03 Async runtime Tokio (current-thread or multi-thread, configurable) async-std, smol, blocking threads Ecosystem fit; structured concurrency; background tasks (consolidation, decay, compaction) as tokio tasks; select! for timeout-guarded LlmIface calls async-std wins if the embedding of this library into a downstream codebase already uses async-std exclusively and runtime mixing is prohibited by policy. Blocking threads wins when the subsystem is embedded in a synchronous CLI tool (mem-cli standalone mode) where no async executor exists and spawn_blocking semantics are cleaner than a full Tokio runtime.
ADR-04 Serialization (WAL + store) bincode (v2, deterministic) serde_json, protobuf, flatbuffers, rkyv Compact binary; deterministic encoding required for CRC32c; zero-copy reads via rkyv considered but bincode simpler for variable-length types protobuf wins when forward/backward compatibility is required across independently versioned services that exchange MemoryRecord over a network boundary (e.g., read-replica replication in PhaseDeferred 16.x). rkyv wins when profiling shows deserialization dominates hot-path latency and the record schema is stable enough to justify zero-copy unsafe read. serde_json wins for human-readable diagnostic dumps and migration tooling where byte efficiency is irrelevant.
ADR-05 On-disk format versioning bincode + manual schema version field in file headers serde with version tags, protobuf field evolution Explicit migration code per version bump; no magic format evolution; migration tested in CI protobuf field evolution wins when the deployment model requires rolling upgrades where multiple versions of the reader may access the same WAL simultaneously (e.g., blue/green deployment of an agent host that reads old WAL entries during the transition window).
ADR-06 ANN / vector index Built-in HNSW (mem-index); pluggable AnnBackend trait FAISS via FFI, hnswlib via FFI, Annoy, ScaNN Avoid unsafe FFI as default; pure-Rust HNSW sufficient for ≤10M vectors; FAISS backend available via feature flag for production scale FAISS via FFI wins when corpus exceeds ~10M vectors and GPU IVFPQ or IVF-Flat compression is required to keep index memory below hardware limits. ScaNN wins when quantization-aware retrieval (asymmetric distance) is the primary latency lever and a C++ interop cost is acceptable. hnswlib via FFI wins when benchmark evidence shows the pure-Rust HNSW implementation is the retrieval bottleneck and the team accepts unsafe FFI under a sandboxed build.
ADR-07 Full-text index BM25 inverted index (hand-rolled, mem-index) tantivy, meilisearch, Lucene FFI tantivy is heavy dep; our BM25 only needs score + posting list; keeps dep surface minimal tantivy wins when requirements expand to include fielded queries, phrase proximity, fuzzy match, facets, or multi-language analysis — features that would require re-implementing significant portions of tantivy's query layer. If the BM25 keyword workload grows beyond simple unigram scoring and requires a full query parser, tantivy's battle-tested segment architecture pays off.
ADR-08 Embedding provider Abstract LlmIface::embed() trait; no default provider OpenAI hard-coded, HuggingFace Transformers, ort (ONNX) Provider-neutral; embedding cache (mem-embed) hides latency; allows local ONNX runtime or remote API ort (ONNX) hard-coded wins when the deployment environment is air-gapped, latency to a remote API is unacceptable (< 5 ms embedding budget), and the embedding model is fixed for the lifetime of the deployment. HuggingFace Transformers hard-coded wins in a research prototype where model fine-tuning iteration velocity matters more than provider flexibility.
ADR-09 LLM interface (summarize, extract, reflect, rerank) LlmIface trait (mem-embed + mem-consolidate) Hardcoded OpenAI/Anthropic SDK, langchain Dependency inversion; tests inject mock LlmIface; production implementations are separate crates Hardcoded OpenAI SDK wins when the agent product exclusively targets the OpenAI platform, zero provider switching is planned, and the team prefers reduced abstraction layers to simplify debugging. LangChain wins when the agent orchestration layer already depends on LangChain and adding another abstraction boundary over an existing one increases rather than decreases complexity.
ADR-10 WAL / durability Append-only WAL with CRC32c per entry; configurable fsync policy SQLite WAL, RocksDB, sled, redb Custom WAL gives exact control over op semantics needed for OP_CONSOLIDATED, OP_EVICT; SQLite too opaque for partial-entry recovery SQLite WAL wins when the team needs a mature, audited, full-ACID embedded store with SQL query access for migration tooling, and is willing to accept SQLite's opacity in exchange for eliminating the custom WAL maintenance burden. RocksDB wins when write amplification at high memory-record throughput (>100k writes/s) makes a single-file WAL a bottleneck, and LSM-tree compaction parallelism is needed.
ADR-11 Record store mmap memmap2 crate for mmap read; pwrite for writes read() syscalls, SQLite blobs mmap amortizes read cost over repeated record fetches in retrieval hot path; writes via pwrite avoid mmap-write complexity read() syscalls win when the deployment environment prohibits mmap (certain seccomp sandbox profiles, strict WASM targets, or operating under SELinux policies that deny mmap of file-backed regions). SQLite blobs win when the record access pattern is highly random-access with no spatial locality, making mmap pre-fetching wasteful, and SQL indexing over blob metadata is preferable.
ADR-12 Memory-safe concurrency tokio::sync::{RwLock, Mutex} for index guards; Arc for shared state std::sync, crossbeam Async-aware locks avoid blocking tokio threads; RwLock allows concurrent readers on HNSW during retrieval std::sync wins when the subsystem is embedded in a synchronous, non-async context (e.g., an FFI call from C) and tokio primitives would require spawning an executor. crossbeam wins when profiling identifies the RwLock as a contention hotspot and epoch-based lock-free data structures provide measurable throughput gains.
ADR-13 Metrics metrics crate (facade) + metrics-exporter-prometheus OpenTelemetry metrics only, statsd, custom metrics facade allows swap of backend; Prometheus pull model suits sidecar deployment OpenTelemetry metrics only wins when the operator requires a single unified telemetry pipeline (metrics + traces + logs via OTLP) and running both a metrics exporter and an OTel metrics exporter is redundant overhead. statsd wins in a legacy infrastructure where the central metrics aggregator is a statsd-compatible collector and Prometheus scraping is unavailable.
ADR-14 Distributed tracing tracing + tracing-opentelemetry + opentelemetry-otlp log crate only, slog, structured println tracing spans correlate across remember/recall/consolidate; OTLP export to Jaeger/Tempo log crate only wins in an embedded microcontroller or resource-constrained environment where the OTel SDK binary footprint (hundreds of KB) is prohibitive. slog wins in a legacy Rust codebase already built around slog where adding a second structured logging framework would fragment log aggregation pipelines.
ADR-15 Logging tracing (unified with tracing above); JSON formatter for prod env_logger, slog Single framework; tracing::instrument decorates every public API call env_logger wins in a standalone CLI tool (mem-cli in debug mode) where structured JSON logs add noise for an operator reading output at the terminal; env_logger's human-readable output is more ergonomic for interactive use.
ADR-16 Testing framework cargo test (unit + integration); tokio::test for async nextest (used in CI for parallelism), pytest Standard; nextest used in CI for speed; proptest for property tests pytest wins if the test surface that matters most is a Python binding layer (e.g., a PyO3-exposed mem-api), where pytest fixtures, parametrize, and the Python ecosystem's test libraries provide richer integration testing than Rust tests against the Python ABI.
ADR-17 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targeting WAL parser, bincode deserializer, query parser AFL++, Honggfuzz Integrates with LLVM coverage; fuzz targets in fuzz/ dir; run in CI nightly AFL++ wins when the team needs grey-box fuzzing with mutation dictionaries tuned for binary protocol formats (custom WAL frame grammar) and finds libFuzzer's mutation engine insufficient for navigating deep state in the WAL parser. Honggfuzz wins when persistent mode and hardware performance counters (branch coverage via perf) are available and show measurably better coverage than libFuzzer on the bincode deserializer target.
ADR-18 Property testing proptest quickcheck proptest shrinking; strategy composition for Query, MemoryRecord; used in mem-retrieve, mem-store quickcheck wins when the property test suite is simple (no complex strategy composition) and the team is already familiar with Haskell-style Arbitrary type class derivation; quickcheck's simpler API reduces onboarding friction in that case.
ADR-19 Benchmark harness criterion (micro); custom soak harness in mem-bench hyperfine (CLI only), divan criterion for regression detection; mem-bench for retrieval quality (nDCG, MRR) + throughput hyperfine wins for wall-clock benchmarking of mem-cli subcommands where the unit under test is a process invocation (e.g., mem-cli compact --dry-run) and micro-level in-process benchmarking via criterion is inapplicable. divan wins if compile times become a significant CI friction point and divan's lighter footprint reduces the benchmark build step measurably.
ADR-20 Dependency policy Minimal; each new dep requires ADR entry; cargo deny enforces license + advisory permissive / no policy Attack surface; compile time; license compliance (MIT/Apache-2.0 only) Permissive policy wins in a rapid-prototype phase where engineering velocity is the only metric, audits are deferred, and the dep surface will be trimmed before production hardening. Permissive policy also wins when the consuming organization has an established SBOM and vulnerability scanning pipeline that makes fine-grained dependency restrictions redundant.
ADR-21 CI/CD GitHub Actions; matrix: stable + MSRV 1.78; cargo deny, cargo audit, cargo clippy --deny warnings Jenkins, CircleCI Standard OSS choice; MSRV matrix catches regression; deny/audit for supply-chain Jenkins wins when the organization's security policy requires CI to run on self-hosted infrastructure behind a corporate firewall with no outbound internet access. CircleCI wins when the team is already invested in CircleCI's orbs ecosystem and migration cost to GitHub Actions exceeds the benefits.
ADR-22 Reproducible builds Cargo.lock committed; cargo vendor for offline; pinned toolchain via rust-toolchain.toml floating deps Reproducibility required for audits; rust-toolchain.toml pins rustc version Floating deps win in an actively developed library where staying current with upstream security patches automatically is more valuable than byte-for-byte build reproducibility, and a downstream application (not the library) owns the Cargo.lock.
ADR-23 Packaging / distribution Published as Cargo crates (crates.io); mem-cli as static binary (musl target) Docker-only, npm wrapper Library use case dominant; CLI binary for ops tooling; musl for portability Docker-only wins when the deployment target is a Kubernetes cluster where operators never install native binaries directly and the container image is the only supported distribution artifact, making crates.io publication irrelevant.
ADR-24 Container / orchestration support Helm chart + K8s Deployment for mem-cli sidecar pattern; optional Kubernetes Operator, docker-compose only Sidecar pattern common for agent deployments; Helm chart is optional convenience Kubernetes Operator wins when the memory subsystem requires lifecycle management beyond simple Deployment semantics — e.g., automated WAL compaction scheduling, rolling schema migrations triggered by CRD version bumps, or cross-namespace secret rotation. docker-compose only wins in a local development or single-node staging environment where Kubernetes overhead is unjustified.
ADR-25 Security tooling cargo audit (RUSTSEC); cargo deny (licenses + advisories); cargo clippy; fuzzing manual review only, Snyk Defense-in-depth; RUSTSEC catches CVEs in deps; clippy catches unsafe patterns Snyk wins when the organization mandates a single centralized SCA (Software Composition Analysis) platform that aggregates findings across multiple languages and repos, and per-language tools like cargo-audit would fragment the security dashboard.
ADR-26 Documentation rustdoc with doctests; FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md as spec; ADR table as this section Sphinx, mdBook rustdoc co-located with code; doctests as lightweight executable specs mdBook wins when the primary audience for documentation is non-Rust engineers (ML engineers, operators) who navigate the docs via a rendered book rather than API reference, and rustdoc's output is too terse for prose explanations of retrieval algorithms and WAL semantics.
ADR-27 Release engineering cargo-release; semver; CHANGELOG.md (Keep a Changelog format) manual tagging, conventional commits tooling cargo-release automates version bump + publish; semver enforced by cargo semver-checks in CI Conventional commits tooling (e.g., release-please) wins when the repository adopts Conventional Commits across all contributors and the changelog must be machine-generated from commit messages rather than maintained manually — common in large teams where manual CHANGELOG.md updates are a recurring source of merge conflicts.
ADR-28 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (SPDX dual) GPL, LGPL, proprietary Maximises downstream adoption; consistent with Rust ecosystem norms; cargo deny verifies transitive deps GPL wins if the project's strategic goal is to ensure derivative works remain open-source (copyleft protection), accepting the constraint that downstream commercial embeddings require a separate commercial license agreement. Proprietary wins if the memory subsystem is a competitive differentiator that the organization intends to monetize via a SaaS offering and open-sourcing the implementation would undermine that business model.
