fixtures/auth/tampered_token.json Valid ns, tampered token (flip bit 7) AccessDenied; no partial result
fixtures/crash/mid_delete_wal.binlog WAL truncated during namespace deletion On recovery: Draining namespace resumes deletion
fixtures/regression/concurrent_writes_10k.jsonl 10K writes from 10 agents Record count in store == 10K; no duplicates

23.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal Recovery
NsHandle lazy-init race Two agents concurrently access new namespace; two NsHandles created entry().or_insert_with() in DashMap not atomic for complex init ns_manager.double_init_total counter Use DashMap::entry(...).or_try_insert_with(async_init_fn) with single-init guarantee
Quota counter divergence after crash Quota allows writes beyond limit AtomicU64 counters not persisted; rebuilt from scan but scan misses un-compacted WAL records quota.rebuild_scan_duration_ms + compare counter vs. store count Add WAL replay to quota rebuild; integration test crash-then-check-quota
Sharing policy cache stale Agent gains access after policy removed SharingPolicy cached in NsHandle; not reloaded on update Log policy update; enforce_access uses stale cache Policy stored in mem-store; hot-reload on config-update notification via channel
Session index bloat (NsId, SessionId) → Vec<MemoryId> grows unbounded for long-lived namespaces Sessions never cleaned up; index not evicted mem-index.session_index_size_bytes gauge TTL sessions; purge session index entries on session close / after inactivity timeout
WAL global lock starvation Namespace B writes blocked by namespace A's large batch Single Mutex<WalWriter> shared store.wal_lock_wait_ms histogram; per-namespace write throughput drops Increase WAL batch window; separate WAL per namespace if throughput bottleneck confirmed
Cross-namespace lock ordering Deadlock when feature requires holding two namespace write locks (e.g., merge-namespace operation) Alphabetic/monotonic lock ordering not enforced Deadlock detector trip Document invariant: never hold > 1 namespace write lock; merge-namespace uses copy+delete pattern
Token secret rotation Old tokens valid after secret rotation NsToken HMAC uses old key; new key not propagated auth.token_verify_fail_total spike post-rotation Support Vec<secret_key> (key ring); verify against all keys; retire old keys after grace period
Namespace deletion leaves orphaned index entries Retrieval returns ghost records from deleted namespace Deletion GC crashes after WAL tombstone but before index cleanup mem-index consistency check on open; index.orphan_ns_entries gauge Index cleanup re-runs on Draining namespace detection at startup

23.7 Performance Concerns

  • Per-namespace RwLock read contention: multiple readers on same namespace proceed in parallel; only writers block. For read-heavy workloads (recall >> remember), read lock is uncontended.
  • Global WAL Mutex: bottleneck at high write concurrency. Mitigate: batch coalescing (up to 64 entries per fsync); saturates at ~20K writes/s on NVMe. If higher needed: per-namespace WAL (revisit rejected alternative).
  • DashMap shard contention for NamespaceManager: DashMap uses 64 shards by default; with 1000 namespaces, ~16 ns/s per shard; negligible.
  • Quota AtomicU64: fetch_add / fetch_sub are lock-free; no contention concern.
  • Session index write on every remember(): B-tree insert O(log M) where M = records per session; for 10K records/session → ~14 ops. Cache session B-tree in NsHandle.
  • Namespace startup scan (quota rebuild): for 1M records → ~500 ms. Run async at startup; mark namespace Initializing until complete; reject writes during init.
  • Cross-namespace reads (sharing policy): no additional lock; sharing enforced at enforce_access() check only; no extra I/O.

23.8 Edge Cases

  • NsId collision: Ulid collision probability negligible; assert uniqueness on creation; MemError::NamespaceExists on duplicate.
  • Empty namespace recall: returns Ok(vec![]) in O(1); no index scan.
  • Namespace created then immediately deleted (< 1 GC cycle): records never stored; deletion completes trivially; tombstone written.
  • Cross-namespace circular sharing: ns_A readable_by ns_B, ns_B readable_by ns_A — not a security issue; sharing is not transitive (A can read B's records but cannot read C's records via B's sharing).
  • Session ID reuse across namespaces: (NsId, SessionId) compound key; same SessionId in different namespaces is distinct; session index keyed by compound.
  • Quota decrease after records exist (operator lowers max_records): existing over-quota records are NOT evicted; only new writes rejected. Log warn quota.retroactive_violation.
  • Zero-quota namespace (max_records=0): every remember() → QuotaExceeded; valid config for read-only shared namespaces.
  • Concurrent namespace delete + active recall: recall acquires read lock; delete waits for exclusive lock; no data corruption; recall completes before delete proceeds.

23.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
NamespaceManager init failure (store unreadable) Startup panics; process exits All namespaces inaccessible Operator restores store; restart WAL replay with checksum; corrupt segments skipped with warning
Sharing policy update lost on crash Cross-namespace access fails after restart Queries denied incorrectly Policy stored in WAL; replayed on startup; if WAL incomplete, policy reverts to pre-update state Re-apply policy update; no data loss
Quota counter over-count (bug in decrement) Namespace appears full while under limit New writes rejected quota.counter_vs_store_delta gauge (periodic reconciliation) Reconcile counters from store scan on operator trigger; mem-cli quota reconcile --ns <id>
Session index corruption Session-scoped recall returns wrong records Queries with session_filter affected mem-index session consistency check Rebuild session index from WAL Provenance.session fields
Token secret lost (no backup) All existing tokens invalid after restart All namespace access denied auth.token_verify_fail_total spikes to 100% Rotate all tokens (re-issue); operator must re-distribute tokens to agents
Per-namespace RwLock write starvation Long-running recall holds read lock; writers blocked indefinitely New memories not stored for affected namespace api.write_wait_ms histogram tail; ns.write_blocked_total counter RwLock with write-preference (or fair variant); cap recall duration

23.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: ns.active_count gauge; ns.record_count{ns_id} gauge; ns.quota_utilization{ns_id} [0,1]; api.access_denied_total{mode}; api.write_wait_ms{ns_id} histogram.
  • Tracing: every remember()/recall() span includes ns_id, session_id; enables per-namespace query analysis.
  • Namespace inspection: mem-cli ns list → all namespaces + status + quota; mem-cli ns inspect --id <NsId> → descriptor, quota, sharing, record count, index sizes.
  • Isolation audit: mem-cli ns audit --id <NsId> → verifies all store records have matching index entries; all index entries reference valid store records; no cross-namespace index leakage.
  • Migration / schema changes: NamespaceDescriptor fields are backward-compatible (additive only); old WAL entries missing new fields use defaults on replay.
  • Rollback: if sharing policy causes unexpected access, set sharing.readable_by = [] via mem-cli ns update; enforced on next API call (no restart).
  • Multi-tenant deployment: enable AuthMode::Token; distribute NsToken to agents via secure channel; rotate tokens on agent compromise with mem-cli ns rotate-token --id <NsId>.
  • Rate limiting: quota is record/byte-count; add NsQuota.max_writes_per_second for future phase if write rate limiting needed (not in scope for Phase 11).

24. Phase 12 — Observability — retrieval quality (recall@k), memory growth, consolidation lag, decay stats, tracing

24.1 Objectives

  • Wire mem-telemetry into every hot path: ingest, retrieval, consolidation, forget, context assembly.
  • Expose recall@k / precision@k metrics computed continuously against a golden fixture set.
  • Track memory growth (record count, WAL bytes, embedding store bytes, index bytes) per namespace.
  • Measure consolidation lag = wall-time between record ingestion and its first consolidation pass.
  • Emit decay / eviction stats: records moved hot→warm, warm→cold, cold→archive, cold→deleted, per tier per minute.
  • Produce structured distributed traces (OpenTelemetry spans) for every remember() / recall() call, correlated by trace_id.
  • All metrics push-able to Prometheus; all traces exportable via OTLP.
  • Constraint: telemetry overhead ≤ 2 % of p99 recall latency at 10 k QPS; no heap alloc per span on hot path (pre-allocated ring of span slots).

24.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P6 (Observability by Default — TelemetrySink wired at startup, no opt-in per crate), P5 (Bounded Resource Usage — pre-allocated SpanGuard ring, ≤2% overhead budget), ADR-13 (metrics crate facade), ADR-14 (tracing + opentelemetry-otlp), ADR-15 (tracing unified logging), S4 (OpenTelemetry spec — semantic conventions, attribute naming).

  • mem-telemetry additions: TelemetrySink trait (record_counter/record_gauge/record_histogram/start_span→SpanGuard); impls PrometheusSink, OtelSink, NoopSink. MetricHandle wraps Arc<dyn TelemetrySink>, cloned into every subsystem at init. SpanGuard is RAII, records duration on drop, carries trace_id/span_id/parent. Hot path uses &'static str metric names, interned tag values — no dynamic alloc.
  • recall@k (mem-bench + mem-telemetry): QualityMonitor (quality.rs) holds a golden Vec<(Query,Vec<MemoryId>)> from fixture JSON; tokio-interval task (default 60s) runs each query against live mem-retrieve, computes recall@k=|retrieved∩relevant|/|relevant| for k∈{1,5,10,20}, emits mem_retrieval_recall_at_k{k,namespace}. PrecisionMonitor analogous, also emits mem_retrieval_mrr.
  • Growth metrics: StorageStats{wal_bytes,record_bytes,embedding_bytes,index_bytes,record_count,namespace} polled every 30s → gauges mem_store_wal_bytes/record_count/embedding_bytes/mem_index_total_bytes; storage_stats(ns) on mem-store, index_bytes() on mem-index.
  • Consolidation lag: mem-ingest stamps ingest_at into a lock-free SPMC PendingConsolidation queue (crossbeam-channel); mem-consolidate pops on job completion, computes lag=completed_at-ingest_at, publishes mem_consolidation_lag_seconds histogram; p95>30s triggers mem_consolidation_lag_breach.
  • Decay/eviction stats: mem-forget emits mem_forget_transition_total{from_tier,to_tier,reason∈[ttl_expired,decay_score,explicit,capacity_pressure]}, mem_forget_archive_bytes gauge, mem_forget_decay_score_distribution histogram sampled per eviction decision.
  • Tracing: remember() root span mem.remember (children: ingest.extract/embed/dedup, store.wal_write); recall() root span mem.recall (children: retrieve.vector/bm25/recency/fusion/rerank, context.assemble); all carry namespace/session_id/trace_id. Context propagated via opentelemetry::Context; injectable via RecallOptions.trace_context.
  • Instrumentation hooks: mem-retrieve/src/hybrid.rs spans each sub-retriever + per-component score histograms; mem-consolidate/src/job.rs spans per job tagged by type; mem-context/src/assembler.rs emits mem_context_tokens_used/mem_context_evictions_working gauges.

24.3 Deliverable

  • mem-telemetry compiles; TelemetrySink injected via DI at startup.
  • Prometheus /metrics endpoint: ≥ 30 named metrics visible after 60 s of traffic.
  • OTLP trace export: Jaeger or OTEL collector receives spans; trace_id correlates remember/recall pairs.
  • QualityMonitor background task running; recall@k gauges updating every 60 s.
  • consolidation_lag_seconds histogram visible; eviction counters incrementing under load.
  • cargo test -p mem-telemetry passes; integration test telemetry_smoke asserts all gauges non-zero after 100 ingests + 10 recalls.

24.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Telemetry overhead (p99 recall) ≤ 2 % at 10 k QPS
recall@10 gauge visible within 120 s of first 50 ingests
Span count per recall() ≥ 6 child spans captured in OTLP
consolidation_lag p95 reported; alert fires if > 30 s
Eviction counters increment on TTL-driven eviction in test
Heap alloc per span (hot path) 0 (verified via DHAT in CI)
cargo test -p mem-telemetry all pass
recall@10 on golden fixture (≥ 200 records) ≥ 0.80

24.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
telemetry/golden_queries.json 20 queries + relevant MemoryId sets recall@10 ≥ 0.80 per query
telemetry/growth_baseline.json record counts + byte sizes after 1 k ingests storage gauges within ±5 % of baseline
telemetry/eviction_sequence.json 50 records with short TTLs mem_forget_transition_total{ttl_expired} = 50
telemetry/trace_capture.jsonl OTLP span export from 10 recall() calls ≥ 6 child spans per trace, trace_id set
telemetry/consolidation_lag_fixture.json 100 rapid ingests; consolidation disabled 20 s p95 lag reported ≥ 18 s
telemetry/malformed_golden.json golden set with unknown MemoryIds QualityMonitor skips unknown ids; no panic
telemetry/no_sink_noop.json NoopSink configured zero overhead; all calls return immediately

24.6 Pitfalls

  • Metric cardinality explosion: namespace tag per-metric; if namespaces are unbounded (user-derived strings), label cardinality blows Prometheus memory. Root cause: unrestricted tag values. Fix: intern NsId to a u32 at registration; cap distinct namespace label values at 256, overflow maps to _overflow.
  • Span context loss across async await: opentelemetry::Context must be explicitly propagated into spawned tasks; default thread-local context lost on .await switch. Manifestation: orphan spans with no parent. Debug: check parent_span_id = 0 in OTLP export. Fix: pass cx: Context explicitly; use attach() guard per task.
  • QualityMonitor golden set staleness: if golden MemoryIds are deleted by forgetting, recall@k silently drops to 0 (false alarm). Manifestation: recall gauge drops without query degradation. Fix: QualityMonitor checks record existence before computing; skips deleted ids; emits mem_quality_monitor_stale_ids counter.
  • Histogram bucket misconfiguration: default exponential buckets may not resolve sub-millisecond latencies. Manifestation: all recall latencies in single bucket. Fix: configure explicit buckets [0.0001, 0.0005, 0.001, 0.005, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.5, 1.0, 5.0] seconds.
  • Consolidation lag queue memory growth: if consolidation stalls, PendingConsolidation queue grows unbounded. Fix: bounded channel (capacity = 100 k); on full, drop oldest and increment mem_consolidation_queue_dropped counter.
  • OTLP export blocking recall path: synchronous OTLP flush blocks hot path. Fix: async OTLP exporter with internal ring buffer; drop spans if ring full rather than block; emit mem_telemetry_spans_dropped counter.
  • Double-counting on retry: if remember() is retried after transient error, ingest_at is re-stamped, inflating lag. Fix: stamp ingest_at only on first enqueue; deduplicate by MemoryId in queue.

24.7 Performance Concerns

  • Pre-allocated span ring: SpanGuard drawn from a thread-local pool of 1024 pre-allocated slots; avoids per-span heap alloc; slot returned on drop.
  • Atomic counters only on hot path: Prometheus counters use AtomicU64::fetch_add; no mutex; no allocation.
  • QualityMonitor off critical path: runs in separate tokio task; golden query set cached; no lock contention with recall().
  • Histogram recording: parking_lot::Mutex on bucket array; contention negligible at 10 k QPS with 1 µs critical section.
  • Eviction counter batching: mem-forget batches eviction events into 100-record chunks before incrementing counter; amortizes atomic ops.
  • Storage stats polling interval: 30 s default; stat() syscall avoided by tracking in-process byte counters incremented on every WAL write.
  • Tail latency: OTLP ring buffer drop policy prevents p99 from being inflated by export backpressure; acceptable span loss ≤ 0.1 % under normal load.

24.8 Edge Cases

  • Namespace deleted mid-poll: storage_stats returns StorageStats::zero(); gauge set to 0, not removed (Prometheus staleness).
  • Recall query on empty store: recall@k = 0.0 (not NaN); denominator guard in QualityMonitor.
  • TraceContext injected with malformed trace_id (not 16 bytes): rejected, new trace_id generated; warning logged.
  • Clock skew between ingest and consolidation workers: lag may be negative if consolidation host clock ahead; clamp to 0 before recording.
  • TelemetrySink init failure (OTLP unreachable): fall back to NoopSink; log warning; do not abort startup.
  • Concurrent namespace creation during quality poll: lock-free read of namespace registry; new namespace appears in next poll cycle.
  • Eviction of a record in PendingConsolidation queue: queue consumer checks record existence; skips if already deleted.

24.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
Prometheus exporter panic mem_telemetry_exporter_errors counter metrics unavailable restart exporter goroutine; NoopSink fallback hot-reload exporter; no data loss
QualityMonitor query timeout mem_quality_monitor_timeout_total recall@k gauge stale skip iteration; retry next interval reduce golden set size
Span ring buffer full mem_telemetry_spans_dropped counter trace gaps drop oldest spans; no functional impact increase ring size; reduce export latency
Consolidation queue full mem_consolidation_queue_dropped counter lag underreported drop oldest entries; alert if drop rate > 1 % increase queue capacity; scale consolidation
OTLP collector unreachable log warning at startup no distributed traces NoopSink active; metrics unaffected reconfigure OTLP endpoint; restart

24.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics endpoint: GET /metrics (configurable port, default 9090); protected by optional bearer token.
  • Dashboards: pre-built Grafana JSON at mem-telemetry/dashboards/; panels: recall@k over time, consolidation lag p50/p95/p99, growth rate (records/min), eviction rate by tier, recall latency heatmap.
  • Alerting rules: mem_consolidation_lag_seconds{quantile="0.95"} > 30 → page; mem_retrieval_recall_at_10 < 0.70 → ticket; mem_forget_transition_total{reason="capacity_pressure"} rate spike → page.
  • Log level: telemetry subsystem logs at WARN and above by default; TRACE enabled per-namespace via env var MEM_TELEMETRY_TRACE_NS=<ns>.
  • Rollout: telemetry is additive; existing store/index/retrieve crates unchanged except for injected MetricHandle; safe zero-downtime deploy.
  • Migration: no schema migration needed; metrics are ephemeral; golden fixture JSON versioned with semver tag in filename.
  • DHAT CI check: nightly job runs cargo test --features dhat-heap -p mem-telemetry; asserts zero allocations on hot span path; blocks merge if violated.

25. Phase 13 — Conformance, differential, fuzzing, soak, benchmarks — vs brute-force kNN; retrieval-quality fixtures; growth/soak tests

25.1 Objectives

  • Conformance suite: verify every invariant from the spine (durability, no-silent-loss, embedding-record consistency, retrieval determinism, context-budget, monotonic ids) via reproducible fixture-driven tests.
  • Differential testing: compare HNSW ANN results against a brute-force exact kNN oracle for every recall query; enforce recall@10 ≥ 0.90 across all fixture corpora.
  • Fuzzing: property-based + libfuzzer targets for WAL replay, query parsing, embedding ingestion, dedup logic, TTL expiry ordering.
  • Soak tests: 24 h continuous ingest + recall at production-representative load; assert no memory leak, no WAL corruption, no recall@k degradation.
  • Benchmarks (mem-bench crate): latency (p50/p95/p99), throughput (records/s ingest; QPS recall), index build time vs N, memory footprint vs N.
  • Constraint: all conformance + differential tests run in cargo test; soak and bench are opt-in via feature flag / separate binary; no external service deps in CI (mock LlmIface).

25.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: P4 (Retrieval Determinism — differential harness validates identical recall for identical state), P14 (Test Fidelity — mock LlmIface, no external service deps in CI), I4 (Retrieval Determinism — invariant fixture asserts deterministic rank), I1 (Durability — WAL replay conformance fixture), I2 (No-Silent-Loss — no-silent-loss conformance fixture), ADR-16 (cargo test + tokio::test), ADR-17 (cargo-fuzz targets), ADR-18 (proptest for conformance), ADR-19 (criterion micro + custom soak), PB14 (Cohere/HuggingFace MTEB 2022 — benchmark suite metrics mapping).

  • mem-bench layout: main.rs (CLI subcommands bench/soak/conformance/differential), bench_ingest.rs (ingest_throughput, embed_cache_hit_rate), bench_retrieve.rs (recall_latency_p99, hybrid_fusion_time, rerank_time), bench_index.rs (hnsw_build_time_vs_n, bm25_build_time_vs_n), bench_context.rs (context_assemble_time, token_budget_overflow), differential.rs, soak.rs, conformance.rs, fuzz_targets/{wal_replay,query_parse,dedup_hash,ttl_eviction}.rs.
  • Brute-force kNN oracle (differential.rs): BruteForceKnn::query(corpus,q,k) — exhaustive cosine, O(N·D), correctness-over-speed reference. DifferentialHarness loads {id,embedding} fixture corpora, runs the same query through HnswIndex::query() and the oracle, computes overlap@k=|hnsw∩bf|/k; asserts ≥0.90 (k=10), ≥0.95 (k=5), ≥0.99 (k=1), reporting diverging ids/scores on failure. Corpus sizes: 1k/10k/100k/500k, one fixture file each.
  • Conformance fixtures (conformance.rs): durability_roundtrip (1000 ingests, WAL truncate+replay, 0 loss); no_silent_loss (500 ingest, forget 50, 450 retrievable + 50-entry eviction log); embedding_record_consistency (post-compaction, 0 dangling embedding_ref); retrieval_determinism (10× identical Query on frozen state, byte-identical ranked lists); context_budget_invariant (2048-token budget, assembled_tokens≤2048, lowest-priority eviction on overflow); monotonic_ids (16-thread 10k concurrent ingest, ULID-monotone ids); as_of_query (ingest T=0..N, as_of=T_k returns only created_at≤T_k); namespace_isolation (ns A/B, 100 each, 0 cross-leak).
  • Fuzz targets: wal_replay.rs (arbitrary WAL byte streams — replay never panics, returns Ok or MemError::Corruption); query_parse.rs (malformed text/NaN embeddings/k=0/k=MAX/empty+conflicting filters — no panic); dedup_hash.rs (arbitrary record pairs — deterministic dedup decision, no false-merge collisions); ttl_eviction.rs (TTL 0/1ns/Duration::MAX/overflow-prone — ordering consistent, no integer overflow). Each runs cargo fuzz run <target> --max-total-time=300 in CI; corpus in fuzz/corpus/<target>/.
  • Soak (soak.rs): SoakConfig{duration,ingest_rate,recall_qps,consolidation_enabled,forget_rate,namespace_count}; N ingest + M recall worker threads plus 1 jemalloc-stats thread (60s sample). Invariant checks every 5min: recall@10≥0.80, WAL CRC valid, record count matches ingested−forgotten−expired. Leak check: RSS at T=0/1h/6h/24h, growth ≤50MB after 1h plateau. Emits soak_report.json.
  • Criterion benches: ingest_throughput (1/10/100/1000 batches, target ≥1000 rec/s single-threaded); recall_latency (10k/100k/500k corpora, target p99≤50ms at 100k); hnsw_build_time_vs_n (1k/10k/100k, wall time + peak RSS); context_assemble_time (50 candidates, 4096-token budget, target ≤5ms). JSON to target/criterion/; CI fails on >10% regression vs stored baseline.
  • MockLlmIface (mem-ingest/src/mock.rs): deterministic hash-derived embeddings, canned summaries/entities; used across conformance/differential/fuzz/soak — no network calls in CI.

25.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p mem-bench --features conformance runs all 8 conformance fixtures; all pass.
  • cargo bench -p mem-bench runs criterion suite; baseline JSON committed; regression check automated.
  • DifferentialHarness runs against 1 k / 10 k / 100 k corpora; overlap@10 ≥ 0.90 reported.
  • Fuzz targets compile; cargo fuzz run wal_replay --max-total-time=300 exits clean.
  • Soak binary mem-bench soak --duration=3600 runs for 1 h; soak_report.json generated.
  • CI pipeline: conformance + differential + bench regression check all green on PR merge.

25.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Conformance fixtures pass 8/8, zero false positives
HNSW vs brute-force overlap@10 ≥ 0.90 at corpus 100 k
HNSW vs brute-force overlap@5 ≥ 0.95 at corpus 100 k
recall_latency p99 at 100 k records ≤ 50 ms
ingest_throughput single-threaded ≥ 1000 records/s
Fuzz targets no panic / UB in 300 s per target
Soak 1 h RSS growth after plateau ≤ 50 MB
Soak recall@10 at 1 h ≥ 0.80 on golden set
Bench regression vs baseline ≤ 10 % degradation blocks PR
as_of conformance exact set returned, 0 post-as-of records

25.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
conformance/durability_roundtrip.json WAL crash + replay 0 record loss (complete entries)
conformance/no_silent_loss.json explicit forget 50/500 eviction log = 50 entries; 450 retrievable
conformance/embedding_consistency.json post-compaction dangle check 0 dangling embedding_refs
conformance/retrieval_determinism.json 10 identical queries ranked lists byte-identical
conformance/context_budget.json overflow scenario assembled_tokens ≤ budget; evictions logged
conformance/monotonic_ids.json 16-thread ingest 10 k IDs ULID-ordered (timestamp monotone)
conformance/as_of_query.json time-travel query T_k only records with created_at ≤ T_k returned
conformance/namespace_isolation.json cross-ns recall 0 records from foreign namespace
differential/corpus_1k.json 1 k embeddings + 100 queries overlap@10 ≥ 0.90
differential/corpus_10k.json 10 k embeddings + 100 queries overlap@10 ≥ 0.90
differential/corpus_100k.json 100 k embeddings + 100 queries overlap@10 ≥ 0.90
fuzz/corpus/wal_replay/ seed WAL byte streams no panic on replay
fuzz/corpus/query_parse/ edge-case Query structs no panic on parse
bench/baselines/recall_latency.json criterion baseline p99 ≤ 50 ms; regression ≤ 10 %

25.6 Pitfalls

  • Brute-force oracle non-determinism: floating-point cosine similarity on x86 vs ARM may differ by ULP; tie-breaking on score alone causes flaky differential. Fix: secondary sort by MemoryId (ULID bytes) as stable tie-break in both oracle and HNSW path (invariant: retrieval determinism).
  • HNSW construction seed sensitivity: HNSW with random layer assignment depends on PRNG seed; different seeds yield different overlap@k. Manifestation: differential test flaky across runs. Fix: fix RNG seed in HnswIndex::build_for_test(seed: u64) constructor used by differential harness; document production uses OS entropy.
  • Fuzz corpus divergence: fuzz corpus accumulated on one OS may not reproduce on another (byte-order, alignment). Fix: commit minimal seed corpus; derive seeds from content hash not filename.
  • Soak test false leak signals: RSS growth may come from kernel page cache, not heap leak. Fix: use jemalloc malloc_stats_print for heap-only measurement; separate from RSS check; assert heap growth ≤ 20 MB.
  • Criterion baseline stale: if committed baseline was produced on a faster machine, PR from slower CI always fails regression check. Fix: store baseline with machine fingerprint; skip regression if fingerprint differs; always update baseline on main merge.
  • Conformance test order dependence: durability_roundtrip leaves a WAL file; next test inherits corrupted state. Fix: each conformance test runs in tempdir; teardown deletes temp dir unconditionally.
  • MockLlmIface non-uniform embeddings: hash-derived embeddings may cluster in embedding space, inflating HNSW recall artificially. Fix: apply random rotation matrix (fixed seed) to spread embeddings uniformly; verify distribution in differential/corpus_1k.json via variance check.
  • TTL arithmetic overflow in fuzzer: Duration::MAX + now overflows in naive expiry check. Fix: saturating add in expiry calculation; fuzz target asserts no panic with Duration::MAX TTL.

25.7 Performance Concerns

  • Brute-force kNN is O(N·D): at N=100 k, D=1536, single query ≈ 150 M multiplies; acceptable in differential test (not on hot path); run with SIMD via f32x8 autovectorization or explicit packed_simd2.
  • Criterion warm-up: benchmark functions require ≥ 3 s warm-up to stabilize JIT-equivalent effects (LLVM code path warming, page faults); default criterion warm-up = 3 s; increase to 5 s for index benchmarks.
  • Soak memory sampling interval: jemalloc stats call holds internal lock briefly (< 1 µs); sample every 60 s; no contention with recall path at 60 s interval.
  • Fuzz target speed: wal_replay fuzzer throughput target ≥ 10 k executions/s; if slower, libfuzzer corpus minimization reduces redundant inputs; use --reduce_inputs=1.
  • Differential corpus loading: 100 k × 1536 f32 = 600 MB; load once per test run, not per query; stored in Arc<Vec<Embedding>> shared across query threads.
  • Context assemble bench: tokenizer called in benchmark; if tokenizer init is slow, use once_cell::Lazy<Tokenizer>; amortize init cost.
  • Bench JSON output: criterion writes per-measurement JSON; at 100 k records × 1000 queries, output files can exceed 1 GB; configure --measurement-time=5 and --sample-size=50 to bound output.

25.8 Edge Cases

  • k = 0 in Query: brute-force returns empty vec; HNSW returns empty vec; differential reports overlap@0 = 1.0 (vacuously true); no panic.
  • k > corpus_size: both oracle and HNSW return all corpus items; overlap = 1.0; no out-of-bounds.
  • All-identical embeddings in corpus: HNSW cannot distinguish; overlap@k undefined; differential marks this corpus as degenerate, skips overlap assert, emits warning.
  • WAL with only a partial header (1 byte): wal_replay fuzzer; must return MemError::Corruption, not panic.
  • Soak test: consolidation job crashes mid-run: soak.rs detects job thread exit, restarts, increments consolidation_restart_count; does not fail soak.
  • Zero-byte embedding in fuzzer: query_parse target; dimension mismatch between query embedding and index dimension; returns MemError::DimensionMismatch.
  • Namespace created then immediately deleted during soak: recall on deleted namespace returns MemError::NamespaceNotFound; no panic; soak handles error and continues.
  • Concurrent compaction + soak ingest: compaction may temporarily reduce record count; soak invariant check uses record_count ± 5 % tolerance window during compaction.

25.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
Differential overlap@10 < 0.90 CI differential test failure HNSW quality regression block merge; investigate HNSW ef_construction param tune ef_construction; re-run differential
Conformance durability_roundtrip fail test assertion in CI data loss risk in production block merge debug WAL fsync policy; review CRC implementation
Fuzz target panic found CI fuzz job crash potential production panic block merge; file bug fix root cause; add minimized input to corpus
Soak RSS growth > 50 MB soak report RSS metric memory leak in production do not ship release profile with heaptrack; fix leak
Bench regression > 10 % CI criterion comparison performance regression block merge; investigate profile with cargo flamegraph; fix hotspot
Soak recall@10 drop < 0.80 soak golden set check retrieval quality regression alert; do not ship investigate consolidation / index rebuild
Conformance retrieval_determinism flaky test flakiness in CI trust in ANN correctness quarantine; investigate tie-break enforce secondary sort by MemoryId everywhere

25.10 Operational Notes

  • CI integration: conformance + differential + fuzz seed check run on every PR (< 10 min total); soak and full bench run nightly on main.
  • Fuzz corpus management: fuzz/corpus/ checked into repo; new crash inputs committed immediately with bug reference; corpus minimized monthly via cargo fuzz cmin.
  • Benchmark baseline update: automated PR created by CI bot on main merge; human approval required if regression detected in any benchmark.
  • Soak report archival: soak_report.json uploaded to CI artifact store with 90-day retention; link posted to PR for manual review before release.
  • Differential corpus versioning: corpus files versioned as corpus_<hash>.json; hash = SHA256 of first 100 embedding vectors; ensures deterministic corpus identity across machines.
  • MockLlmIface documentation: mem-ingest/src/mock.rs has #[doc = "TEST ONLY"] attribute; deny(dead_code) lint suppressed for mock module under #[cfg(test)].
  • Rollback: conformance and bench tests are read-only; safe to run against any store version; differential test loads corpus independently; no migration needed.
  • Tracing in tests: conformance tests emit spans to NoopSink by default; set MEM_TEST_OTLP=1 to export to local OTLP collector for debugging flaky conformance failures.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries

Boundary Trust Level Crossing Controls
Agent process → mem-api Caller-trusted; auth via NsId token Namespace isolation; no cross-ns read without explicit grant
mem-api → LlmIface External RPC; treat responses as untrusted data Response schema validation; size caps; timeout; retry limits
mem-embed provider Third-party binary/FFI Sandboxed thread; panic-catch; embedding dim/norm validation
WAL / mmap files OS filesystem Checksums per WAL frame; mmap access restricted to store process
mem-cli → mem-api Operator; elevated but audited All destructive ops require explicit confirmation flag; audit log
Backup/restore path Admin Signature verification on snapshot before replay

Attack Surface Inventory

Vector Class Mitigation Detection Recovery
Malformed content string (oversized) DoS / OOM Max content bytes cap (configurable, default 64 KB) before ingest mem_ingest_rejected_oversized_total counter Reject + log; caller receives MemError::PayloadTooLarge
Malformed embedding response (wrong dim, NaN, Inf) Corruption Dimension assert + NaN/Inf check in mem-embed before store mem_embed_invalid_response_total Return MemError::EmbeddingInvalid; do not write record
Replay of old remember() calls (duplicate injection) Data integrity Content-hash dedup in mem-ingest; MemoryId ULID monotone prevents id collision Dedup counter spike; duplicate provenance log Dedup silently absorbs; original record unchanged
Namespace escape via forged NsId Privilege escalation NsId validated against authenticated session token at mem-api boundary mem_api_ns_auth_failure_total; structured log with client id Reject request; revoke session token if repeated
Resource exhaustion: recall() fanout (k=10000) DoS Query.k capped at max_k (default 200); per-session rate limiter Latency p99 spike; mem_retrieve_k_clamped_total Clamp silently; telemetry alert at threshold
Resource exhaustion: consolidation loop starvation DoS Consolidation token bucket; max consolidation jobs in flight mem_consolidate_queue_depth gauge Shed oldest jobs; alert operator
Crafted BM25 query (regex/term explosion) CPU DoS Term count limit (default 64 terms); no regex in BM25 query path mem_index_bm25_terms_clamped_total Clamp; return partial results
WAL segment injection (tampered file) Persistence corruption Per-frame CRC32C + magic header; frame sequence monotone check on recovery Recovery error counter; mem_store_wal_corrupt_frames_total Skip corrupt frame; mark segment needs manual review; alert
mmap OOB via crafted record offset Memory corruption Bounds-checked mmap read wrappers; no raw pointer arithmetic outside unsafe block with documented invariant Crash/SIGBUS caught by process monitor Restart; WAL replay; report corrupted page range
LlmIface prompt injection (agent content → LLM) Data exfiltration / manipulation LlmIface prompt template is static; agent content placed in delimited data field, never in instruction section Anomalous LLM output schema (fails parse) Reject extracted facts; log raw LLM response for audit
Supply-chain: malicious crate dependency Code exec cargo vet; pinned lockfile; reproducible builds; RUSTSEC advisory automation CI diff on Cargo.lock; advisory scan Pin last known-good; quarantine update
Operator mistake: mem-cli forget --all Data loss Requires --confirm-namespace=<nsid> flag; writes tombstone log before deletion Audit log entry; metrics drop Restore from snapshot; replay WAL delta
Embedding provider credential leak Credential theft Credentials injected via env / secret store; never stored in MemoryRecord or WAL Secret scanner in CI; no credential in logs Rotate credential; invalidate cached embeddings if provider changes
Time-travel query poisoning (future as_of) Invariant violation as_of > now() clamped to now() in mem-retrieve before index scan mem_retrieve_asof_clamped_total Clamp; no error; log at debug
Excessive TTL=0 records (instant-expire flood) Store bloat Minimum TTL enforced (default 1 s); expire at ingest if TTL < min Ingest rejection counter Reject; return MemError::TtlTooShort
Consolidation LLM hallucination (invented links) Graph corruption Reflection output validated: MemoryIds in links must exist in store; unknown ids dropped mem_consolidate_invalid_links_dropped_total Drop invalid links; log source reflection job id

Privilege Escalation Paths

  • NsId tokens scoped per session; no super-token except operator CLI with audit trail.
  • mem-embed FFI: runs in isolated thread pool; panics caught, do not propagate to caller stack.
  • Background workers (consolidate, forget) inherit store handle only; no API token; cannot initiate outbound LLM calls with agent credentials.

Persistence Corruption Risk Register

Risk Probability Impact Control
WAL partial write (power loss mid-frame) Medium Single record lost Trailing CRC; incomplete frame discarded on recovery
Compaction crash mid-rename Low Stale snapshot; WAL replay covers delta Atomic rename; old snapshot retained until new one fsync'd
mmap dirty page evicted before fsync Low Record visible but not durable msync(MS_SYNC) before WAL ack; WAL is authoritative
Index-store divergence post-crash Low Stale index entry Index rebuilt from WAL replay on recovery; rebuild tagged in telemetry

Failure Modes & Recovery

Failure Taxonomy

F1 — WAL Write Failure

Attribute Detail
Symptoms remember() returns MemError::StorageWrite; WAL file size stops growing
Root causes Disk full; filesystem error; fd exhaustion; permissions change
Detection WAL write error metric mem_store_wal_write_errors_total; structured log WARN
Automatic recovery Retry with exponential backoff (3×, max 200 ms); if disk-full, trigger compaction to free space
Manual recovery Operator: free disk space; mem-cli store compact --namespace <ns>; restart process
Forensic artifacts Last successful WAL sequence number in log; OS errno in structured log
Long-term mitigation Disk usage alert at 80% capacity; pre-allocate WAL segment headroom

F2 — WAL Recovery Divergence (Partial Frames)

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Startup recovery log shows corrupt_frames > 0; some records missing post-restart
Root causes Power loss mid-frame write; kernel buffer not flushed
Detection mem_store_wal_corrupt_frames_total > 0 on startup; recovery audit log
Automatic recovery Skip corrupt frame; rebuild index from clean frames; records in corrupt frame are lost (last-record durability window)
Manual recovery Inspect last committed frame sequence; cross-check with application-level idempotency key if available
Forensic artifacts WAL segment file; frame sequence gap in recovery log
Long-term mitigation Tune fsync policy; double-buffer WAL with sequence-number ack

F3 — Index-Store Divergence

Attribute Detail
Symptoms recall() returns records not in store (dangling index entry); or store has records not in any index (recall miss)
Root causes Compaction crash between index update and store update; bug in atomic compaction
Detection mem_index_dangling_entries_total (background scan); recall returns record with missing content
Automatic recovery Background reconciliation scan: for each index entry, verify store entry exists; remove dangling; for unindexed store records, re-index
Manual recovery mem-cli index rebuild --namespace <ns>; expects store as authoritative source
Forensic artifacts Compaction log; last compaction checkpoint file
Long-term mitigation Atomic compaction with two-phase commit pattern; post-compaction reconciliation scan

F4 — Embedding Provider Failure

Attribute Detail
Symptoms remember() latency spike or MemError::EmbeddingUnavailable; no new records indexed for vector ANN
Root causes Provider API down; credential expired; network partition; rate limit
Detection mem_embed_provider_errors_total; latency p99 alert
Automatic recovery Retry with jitter (3×); fall back to cached embedding if content hash matches; if no cache, store record without embedding_ref (partial record, BM25-only retrievable)
Manual recovery Rotate credential; switch provider; mem-cli embed backfill --namespace <ns> to re-embed unembedded records
Forensic artifacts Provider error code + HTTP status in structured log
Long-term mitigation Multi-provider fallback chain in mem-embed; local embedding model as last resort

F5 — LlmIface Failure (Extraction / Consolidation)

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Fact extraction skipped (raw content stored without structured facts); consolidation jobs stall
Root causes LLM provider down; token limit exceeded; malformed response
Detection mem_ingest_extraction_skipped_total; mem_consolidate_llm_errors_total
Automatic recovery Ingest: store raw content with extraction-pending flag; retry extraction asynchronously when provider recovers. Consolidation: re-queue job with backoff
Manual recovery mem-cli consolidate retry-pending --namespace <ns>
Forensic artifacts Extraction-pending flag on MemoryRecord; consolidation job state in job store
Long-term mitigation Extraction failure does not block durability; record is always stored

F6 — HNSW Index Corruption

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Vector recall returns empty or nonsensical results; ANN accuracy metrics drop
Root causes mmap corruption; partial write during index update; software bug
Detection mem_index_ann_accuracy_p50 drops below threshold; checksums on HNSW segment fail
Automatic recovery Mark HNSW index as invalid; disable vector retrieval; fall back to BM25 + recency only
Manual recovery mem-cli index rebuild --type hnsw --namespace <ns>; rebuild from stored embeddings
Forensic artifacts HNSW segment checksum log; last successful accuracy measurement
Long-term mitigation Periodic accuracy probe against held-out recall fixture; shadow rebuild on idle

F7 — Context Budget Overflow

Attribute Detail
Symptoms mem_context_overflow_evictions_total counter nonzero; working memory records unexpectedly promoted to long-term
Root causes Token estimator miscalibration; agent pushes large working memory without clearing
Detection Counter spike; working memory size gauge at max
Automatic recovery Evict lowest-salience working memories to episodic/long-term; assemble context within budget
Manual recovery Agent should call forget() on stale working memories; reduce ContextBudget.max_tokens if overallocated
Forensic artifacts Eviction log with MemoryId + reason + salience
Long-term mitigation Calibrate token estimator per model; expose context_headroom metric to agent

F8 — Decay / Eviction Incorrect (Premature Loss)

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Agent reports important memories missing; mem_forget_evictions_total spike without operator action
Root causes Decay model miscalibrated (too aggressive); salience not updated on access; TTL set too short by caller
Detection Eviction audit log (every eviction logged with reason + salience + access_count)
Automatic recovery None automatic; evicted records are archived, not deleted immediately (cold tier, configurable retention)
Manual recovery mem-cli forget restore --id <MemoryId> from cold archive; recalibrate decay parameters
Forensic artifacts Eviction log; cold-tier archive file
Long-term mitigation Eviction dry-run mode; salience floor for recently-accessed records

F9 — Namespace Isolation Breach

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Agent retrieves records from another namespace
Root causes Bug in NsId filter in query path
Detection Cross-namespace retrieval audit (query log annotated with ns); integration test fixture
Automatic recovery None; isolation is an invariant violation
Manual recovery Incident: invalidate all sessions; audit retrieval logs for scope of breach; patch and redeploy
Forensic artifacts Query audit log with namespace assertion
Long-term mitigation NsId predicate enforced at lowest index-scan layer, not only at API layer; fuzz test namespace boundary

F10 — Consolidation Graph Cycle

Attribute Detail
Symptoms Reflection produces MemoryRecord.links that form a cycle; graph traversal infinite loop risk
Root causes LLM hallucination; bug in link extraction
Detection Cycle detection (DFS) on link graph after each consolidation batch; mem_consolidate_cycles_broken_total
Automatic recovery Break cycle at lowest-salience edge; log broken edges
Manual recovery mem-cli graph check --namespace <ns> to audit link graph
Forensic artifacts Cycle detection log with node ids
Long-term mitigation Link graph stored as DAG; insertion validates no cycle created

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy

Metric Type Labels Alert Threshold
mem_remember_latency_ms Histogram ns, kind p99 > 500 ms
mem_recall_latency_ms Histogram ns p99 > 200 ms
mem_store_wal_size_bytes Gauge ns > 80% disk quota
mem_store_wal_write_errors_total Counter ns > 0 in 5 min
mem_index_ann_accuracy_p50 Gauge ns < 0.85 (recall@10)
mem_embed_provider_errors_total Counter provider > 5 in 1 min
mem_consolidate_queue_depth Gauge ns > 500
mem_forget_evictions_total Counter ns, reason spike > 2× baseline
mem_context_overflow_evictions_total Counter ns > 0 in 5 min
mem_ingest_extraction_skipped_total Counter ns > 0
mem_index_dangling_entries_total Gauge ns > 0
mem_store_compaction_duration_ms Histogram ns p99 > 30 s
mem_api_ns_auth_failure_total Counter client > 10 in 1 min
mem_store_record_count Gauge ns, kind informational
mem_embed_cache_hit_ratio Gauge — < 0.5 warning

All metrics exposed via Prometheus-compatible /metrics endpoint (push-mode also supported via OTLP).

Tracing Strategy

  • Every remember() and recall() call opens a root span with mem.namespace, mem.kind, mem.operation.
  • Child spans: extraction, embed, WAL write, index update, vector ANN search, BM25 search, RRF fusion, MMR, rerank, context pack.
  • Sampling: 100% for errors; 1% for success in production; 100% in staging.
  • Span attributes carry memory_id, query_k, score_components, tokens_packed.
  • Consolidation and forget jobs emit job-scoped traces with parent consolidation_job_id.
  • Propagate W3C traceparent header from agent if provided.

Structured Logging

  • All log lines: JSON; mandatory fields: ts, level, crate, ns, operation, memory_id (when applicable), latency_ms (when applicable).
  • Log levels: ERROR for invariant violations and unrecoverable errors; WARN for retried operations and degraded fallbacks; INFO for major lifecycle events (startup, compaction, snapshot); DEBUG for per-record operations (disabled in production by default).
  • Audit log stream (separate sink): every eviction, every NS auth failure, every destructive CLI command.
  • No PII / raw memory content at INFO or above; content logged only at TRACE (disabled by default; gated behind --allow-content-trace flag).

Dashboard Design

Row 1: Ingest rate (rpm) | Recall rate (rpm) | p50/p99 latency (remember/recall)
Row 2: WAL size % quota  | Index accuracy p50 | Consolidation queue depth
Row 3: Embed errors/min  | Eviction rate/min  | Context overflow/min
Row 4: Record count by kind (stacked) | Salience distribution histogram
Row 5: Top namespaces by record count | Auth failures/min

Alerting Philosophy

  • Page (PagerDuty/Opsgenie): WAL write error; namespace isolation breach; index-store divergence confirmed; disk > 90% quota.
  • Ticket (auto-create): embed provider errors sustained > 5 min; ANN accuracy below threshold; consolidation queue > 500 sustained 15 min.
  • Informational (Slack): compaction ran > 20 s; eviction spike > 2× 1-hour baseline.
  • No alert storms: all counters de-duplicated with 5-min cool-down window.

SLO Definitions

SLO Target Measurement Window Budget
remember() success rate 99.9% 30-day rolling 43 min/month error budget
recall() p99 latency ≤ 200 ms 30-day rolling measured at API layer
Durability (ack'd records survive restart) 99.99% Per-deploy validation WAL replay test
Context assembly correctness (budget never exceeded) 100% Continuous integration invariant Zero tolerance
ANN recall@10 accuracy ≥ 0.85 Weekly accuracy probe Below threshold → ticket

Incident Response

  1. Detect: alert fires or on-call observes metric anomaly.
  2. Triage: check dashboard row 1–3; identify failing subsystem by crate-level error counters.
  3. Contain: if data-loss risk: set store to read-only (mem-cli store pause-writes --namespace <ns>); snapshot immediately.
  4. Diagnose: pull structured logs for affected namespace + time window; correlate with trace ids.
  5. Mitigate: apply runbook action (see below).
  6. Recover: verify metrics return to baseline; re-enable writes.
  7. Post-mortem: document root cause, detection gap, mitigation, long-term fix within 5 days.

Runbooks

Scenario Command(s)
Disk full — free space fast mem-cli store compact --namespace <ns> --aggressive
WAL corrupt frames on restart mem-cli store recover --skip-corrupt --namespace <ns>
Rebuild all indices mem-cli index rebuild --all --namespace <ns>
Backfill missing embeddings mem-cli embed backfill --namespace <ns>
Restore evicted record from archive mem-cli forget restore --id <MemoryId>
Pause ingest during incident mem-cli store pause-writes --namespace <ns>
Trigger snapshot mem-cli store snapshot --namespace <ns> --output <path>
Verify snapshot integrity mem-cli store verify-snapshot --path <path>

Backup Strategy

  • Incremental WAL shipping: every closed WAL segment shipped to object storage (S3-compatible) within 60 s; retention 30 days.
  • Full snapshots: daily; kept 7 days local; 30 days remote.
  • Snapshot = mmap store file + HNSW index segment + BM25 index segment + metadata; all three must be consistent (same WAL sequence watermark).
  • Backup integrity: every snapshot includes SHA-256 manifest; restore verifies manifest before replay.

Restore Testing

  • Weekly automated restore drill: restore last daily snapshot into staging; replay WAL delta; run recall fixture suite; assert accuracy ≥ 0.85.
  • Drill result posted to ops dashboard; failure pages on-call.
  • Test covers: full restore, point-in-time restore (as-of query), partial namespace restore.

Deployment Topology

Show the original text diagram
Single-node:  agent process -> mem-api (in-process or local Unix socket)
Multi-tenant: mem-api server (HTTP/gRPC) -> mem-store (per-namespace WAL) -> shared mem-index pool
HA:           Primary mem-store (writes) + read replicas (WAL streaming); recall served from replicas

Canary and Rolling Upgrade Strategy

  • New binary deployed to 5% of namespaces first (canary); monitor p99 latency + error rate 30 min.
  • On canary success: rolling upgrade 25% → 50% → 100% with 10-min holds.
  • WAL format version checked on startup; new binary refuses to open WAL written by future-version binary.
  • On canary failure: automated rollback to previous binary; WAL unchanged (backward-compatible).

Rollback Strategy

  • Binary rollback: replace binary; restart; WAL replay recovers state (WAL format backward-compatible within major version).
  • Index rollback: indices are derived from WAL; rebuilding is always safe and deterministic.
  • Data rollback: restore from snapshot + WAL delta; point-in-time granularity = WAL segment boundary (≤ 60 s).

Migration Strategy

  • Schema migrations run lazily on first access of a record (forward migration) or eagerly via mem-cli store migrate --namespace <ns>.
  • Migration is idempotent; safe to run multiple times.
  • Old schema version readable by new binary; new schema version unreadable by old binary (triggers startup error with clear message pointing to migration command).

Compatibility & Versioning

API Stability (mem-api public surface)

Symbol Stability Policy
remember(), recall(), forget(), reflect() Stable No breaking change without major version bump
Query, RetrievalResult, MemoryRecord, MemError Stable Additive fields allowed; removal = major version
ContextBudget, NsId, MemoryId, MemoryKind Stable No change without major version
LlmIface trait Stable Adding optional methods allowed; removing = major
ConsolidationJob, internal scheduler types Unstable May change in minor versions
mem-cli commands Stable (flags) New flags additive; existing flag removal = deprecation cycle

Wire Compatibility (HTTP/gRPC surface, if mem-api server mode)

  • Protobuf schema: fields added with new field numbers; old fields never reused; unknown fields ignored by receiver.
  • Semantic versioning on API endpoint path (/v1/...); v1 maintained for 12 months after v2 GA.
  • Response envelope includes api_version field; clients can assert version match.

Storage Compatibility

Component Guarantee
WAL frame format Backward-compatible within major version; magic + version header checked on open
mmap record store Forward-migratable via lazy schema upgrade; old binary refuses future schema version
HNSW index segment Rebuilt from embeddings; no cross-version binary compatibility guaranteed; always rebuilable
BM25 inverted index Rebuilt from WAL replay; no cross-version guarantee
Snapshot manifest Semantic versioning; restore tool reads all supported manifest versions

Schema Evolution Rules

  • MemoryRecord: new optional fields added in minor versions; removal requires deprecation period ≥ 2 minor versions.
  • MemoryKind enum: new variants added in minor versions; old binaries receiving unknown variant fall back to Semantic.
  • MemError enum: new variants added freely; callers must handle unknown variants as MemError::Unknown.
  • Query fields: new optional fields (with sensible defaults) added in minor; required field addition = major.

Migration Guarantees

  • Upgrade path: N → N+1 always supported (lazy migration); N → N+2 supported via mem-cli store migrate; N → N+3 requires intermediate step (documented).
  • Downgrade path: not guaranteed after migration; snapshot pre-migration preserved for 7 days.
  • Namespace data portable across hosts via snapshot + WAL bundle; no host-specific encoding.

Deprecation Policy

  • API deprecation: announced in CHANGELOG; deprecated symbol marked #[deprecated] in Rust; removed after ≥ 2 minor-version cycle (≥ 6 months).
  • WAL format deprecation: old format supported for 1 major version after new format GA; migration command provided.
  • CLI flag deprecation: warn on use for ≥ 2 minor versions before removal.
  • LlmIface trait method deprecation: default implementation provided for old method; removed in next major.

Semantic Versioning Policy

Change type Version bump
New stable API symbol Minor
Additive field in stable type Minor
New MemoryKind variant Minor
New WAL feature flag (backward-compat) Minor
Removal / rename of stable API symbol Major
WAL format incompatible change Major
MSRV bump Minor (with 2-release notice)
Behavior change (same API, different result) Minor (documented) or Major (if breaking contract)

Rolling Upgrade Guarantees

  • Primary + replica (HA topology): replica at N-1 can read WAL from primary at N (WAL backward-compatible within major).
  • Mixed-version cluster window: ≤ 30 minutes supported (time-to-complete rolling upgrade); beyond that, behavior undefined.
  • Index divergence during rolling upgrade: read replicas serve stale index during upgrade window; as_of queries remain consistent; ANN accuracy may degrade transiently.
  • After upgrade: replicas rebuild index from WAL; mem_index_rebuild_in_progress gauge signals clients to tolerate temporary accuracy degradation.

Pluggable ANN Backend Compatibility

  • AnnBackend trait: stable; implementations (HNSW built-in, FAISS FFI, hnswlib FFI) swappable via config.
  • Switching ANN backend requires index rebuild; no cross-backend index file format compatibility.
  • Built-in HNSW implementation: index file format versioned independently; version header checked on load.

Testing & Verification Strategy

Every layer below exists to exercise the System Invariants (§7, I1–I12) and each phase's N.5 Conformance Fixtures; a fixture that cannot be traced to an invariant or an acceptance criterion is noise, not verification.

Unit Tests — per-crate, hermetic

Crate Focus Key fixtures
mem-core type invariants: Salience in [0,1], Ulid monotonicity, MemError mapping round-trip serde, boundary values
mem-store WAL write → read, compaction idempotency, snapshot consistency temp-dir fixtures, in-proc crash simulation
mem-index HNSW k-NN correctness (brute-force oracle), BM25 score monotonicity, recency ordering synthetic embeddings, canned corpora
mem-embed chunker split invariants, embedding cache hit/miss, provider mock fixed-dim mock embedder, deterministic output
mem-ingest dedup logic, entity extraction pipeline, observation buffer draining LlmIface stub returning canned facts
mem-retrieve RRF score formula, MMR diversity property, component score decomposition preloaded index fixtures
mem-context budget invariant: assembled tokens ≤ max_tokens, eviction ordering token-count mock
mem-forget decay formula monotonicity, TTL expiry, eviction reason log wall-clock injection
mem-consolidate merge/dedup idempotency, reflection produces valid MemoryRecord mock LlmIface summaries
mem-api session isolation, namespace non-leakage, remember→recall round-trip ephemeral store

Integration Tests — cross-crate paths

  • Write path: mem-ingest → mem-store → mem-index; verify record retrievable within same process.
  • Read path: mem-retrieve → mem-index → mem-store; verify RetrievalResult score components sum consistently.
  • Context assembly: mem-retrieve → mem-context; budget invariant holds under adversarial large records.
  • Forget integration: explicit forget() removes from store AND all indices atomically; no dangling index entry.
  • Consolidation cycle: background worker completes one pass; source records linked to merged record.
  • Crash-recovery: inject panic mid-WAL-write; replay restores pre-crash consistent state; no partial records.
  • Namespace isolation: two NsIds share no records across remember→recall cycle.
  • Time-travel / as-of: records created after as_of timestamp invisible to query.

Property-Based Tests (proptest / quickcheck)

  • ∀ MemoryRecord r: store(r) → load(r.id) == r (store round-trip).
  • ∀ query Q, index I: retrieve(Q, I) is deterministic (stable tie-break by score then MemoryId).
  • ∀ batch of records: compaction(compaction(batch)) == compaction(batch) (idempotent compaction).
  • ∀ embedding e, k: hnsw.search(e, k) ⊆ brute_force(e, k) with recall ≥ 0.95 (parameterized).
  • ∀ salience s ∈ [0,1]: decay(s, t) ≤ s (monotone decay).
  • ∀ context budget B, record set R: assemble(R, B).tokens ≤ B.max_tokens.
  • Shrinking enabled; seed corpus from recorded production-like queries.

Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libfuzzer targets)

Target Input surface Invariant checked
fuzz_wal_replay arbitrary WAL byte stream no panic, deterministic output or MemError
fuzz_query_parse arbitrary query bytes no panic; valid Query or parse error
fuzz_embedding_cache arbitrary byte keys + embeddings no corruption of cached entries
fuzz_hnsw_insert random f32 vectors graph connectivity invariant holds
fuzz_ingest_pipeline arbitrary UTF-8 observation strings no panic; idempotent on duplicate
  • CI runs fuzz targets for 60 s per target per PR; 24 h nightly corpus expansion.
  • Corpus stored in mem-bench/fuzz/corpus/; regressions checked in.

Deterministic Replay / Chaos

  • Replay harness: record real sessions as event log (mem-cli record); replay at test time; compare final store state byte-for-byte.
  • Fault injection: FaultInjector trait wraps mem-store I/O; inject: partial write, fsync failure, torn page, disk-full.
  • Chaos test matrix: WAL fsync fail → verify no silent loss; compaction abort mid-flight → consistent index; index rebuild fail → store still readable (degraded, no retrieval).
  • Concurrency chaos: tokio spawn storm of parallel remember() + recall() + forget() calls; check no deadlock, no record loss, no panic.

Soak / Long-Running Tests

  • 24 h soak: continuous ingest (1 k records/min), retrieve (500 QPS), consolidation running; monitor memory growth (RSS), WAL size, index size.
  • Leak detector: RUST_LOG=mem_store=trace + heaptrack; RSS must not grow unboundedly.
  • Index fragmentation: after 1 M inserts + 10% deletes, HNSW recall must remain ≥ 0.90.

Conformance / Compatibility Testing

  • mem-embed conformance: given identical input, two provider implementations return embeddings within cosine distance 0.001 of reference (providers tested in isolation).
  • Snapshot format compatibility: snapshot v1 readable by v1 + v2 binaries; migration path tested in CI.
  • API surface: mem-api conformance suite validates remember/recall/forget/reflect contracts; implementors of alternative backends run same suite.

Performance-Regression Testing

  • mem-bench crate runs on every PR via CI; results compared to main-branch baseline stored in bench-baseline.json.
  • Regression threshold: p50 latency +10%, p99 +20%, throughput −10% — any breach blocks merge.
  • Tracked metrics: remember_p50, recall_p99, consolidation_throughput, context_assemble_p50, hnsw_recall_at_10.

Build & Release Engineering

Reproducible Builds

  • Cargo.lock committed; all transitive deps pinned.
  • Builds run in hermetic Docker image: ghcr.io/project-327/builder:rustX.Y.Z-alpineN (digest-pinned).
  • SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set to git commit timestamp; no embedded wall-clock in artifacts.
  • RUSTFLAGS="-C debuginfo=0 -C codegen-units=1" for release; determinism verified with diffoscope on two independent CI builds.
  • Build cache keyed on Cargo.lock + rustc --version hash; cache busted on toolchain pin change.

Toolchain Pinning

rust-toolchain.toml:
  channel = "1.78.0"   # MSRV == release toolchain
  components = ["rustfmt", "clippy", "rust-src", "llvm-tools-preview"]
  • Nightly used only in fuzz targets; pinned via separate rust-toolchain.toml in mem-bench/fuzz/.
  • Toolchain upgrade PRs run full test + bench matrix before merge.

CI/CD Topology

Stage Trigger Jobs
PR gate push to PR fmt, clippy (−deny warnings), unit tests, integration tests, 60 s fuzz, bench regression, SBOM diff
Merge to main merge all above + soak smoke (5 min), doc build, artifact publish to staging
Nightly cron 02:00 UTC 24 h soak, full corpus fuzz, full bench baseline update
Release tag vX.Y.Z reproducible release build, SBOM generation, artifact signing, publish
  • CI runner: GitHub Actions; self-hosted runner for soak (persistent disk).
  • Matrix: [linux-x86_64, linux-aarch64, macos-x86_64]; Windows in best-effort matrix.
  • Parallelism: crate-level test partitioning (cargo nextest --partition count:N/M).

Artifact Signing and Provenance

  • Release binaries signed with cosign (keyless, OIDC-backed); signature stored in OCI registry alongside artifact.
  • SLSA provenance level 2: build provenance attestation generated by slsa-github-generator; attached to each GitHub Release.
  • cargo-auditable embeds dependency tree in ELF/Mach-O binary; extractable with rust-audit-info.
  • Checksums: sha256 + blake3 published in checksums.txt alongside each release asset.

SBOM Generation

  • cargo cyclonedx --format json generates CycloneDX SBOM per release; stored in dist/sbom.json.
  • SBOM diff on every PR: new transitive dep or license change surfaces as CI warning.
  • License policy enforced: cargo-deny check licenses (allowlist: MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-2, BSD-3, ISC); deny list: GPL, AGPL, SSPL, proprietary.
  • Vulnerability scan: cargo audit on every PR + nightly; critical CVEs block merge.

Dependency Auditing and Supply-Chain

  • cargo-deny: license + ban + advisories + sources checks; config in deny.toml.
  • All crates sourced from crates.io only (no git deps, no path deps outside workspace) in release builds.
  • cargo-vet: supply-chain audit records in supply-chain/; audits checked in and reviewed in PRs.
  • Renovate bot: weekly automated dep-bump PRs; pinned digest for Docker builder image.

Version Stamping

  • Version: workspace.version in root Cargo.toml; semver; all member crates inherit.
  • Build metadata embedded: env!("VERGEN_GIT_SHA"), env!("VERGEN_BUILD_TIMESTAMP") via vergen in mem-cli and mem-api.
  • Changelog: CHANGELOG.md updated per release using git-cliff; conventional commits enforced via commitizen hook.

Release Channels

Channel Branch Cadence Consumers
